- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: error copy says 'Anthropic request failed' / 'Unknown Anthropic error'

### PRV-006 — The 'thinking' SSE event is declared in the wire contract and handled by the client but nothing server-side ever emits it

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-SAFE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/lib/enhance/stream-events.ts:56-57 declares `{ type: "thinking"; text: string }`; src/lib/enhance/use-enhance.ts:165-167 handles it. src/app/api/enhance/route.ts emits only status/delta/usage/done/error (lines 244-341), and no adapter yields thinking text (ProviderStreamChunk in errors.ts:29-36 has no thinking field). Dead wire grammar — either reserved forward-compat that should say so, or removable.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/enhance/stream-events.ts, src/lib/enhance/use-enhance.ts; no runtime effect today
- **proposed_fix:** Add a comment marking the event as reserved (never yet emitted) or remove the variant and the client case; comment-only is the zero-risk minimum.
- **verification:** grep -rn '"thinking"' src/ shows the variant documented as reserved or gone; typecheck stays green
- **independently found by:** MOD ("The 'thinking' stream event is declared in the SSE wire contract and handled by the client, but no producer in")
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: the thinking event is documented RESERVED in stream-events.ts (kept for a future adapter, zero-risk)

### PRV-007 — Three targets ship floating provider aliases (deepseek-chat, mistral-large-latest, qwen-max) so an upstream model swap silently changes behavior and invalidates the target's price table

- severity `S3` · confidence `likely` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/lib/providers/config.ts:78 'deepseek-chat' ('tracks the current flagship'), :112 'mistral-large-latest', :134 'qwen-max' ('tracks the current Max release') — all three comments acknowledge the drift and defer to env pinning (FINAL_PLAN D9). The remaining 13 ids are undated product names (claude-opus-5, gpt-5.6-sol, gemini-3.6-flash, MiniMax-M3, kimi-k3, sonar-pro, grok-4.5, glm-5.2, muse-spark-1.1), which drift more slowly but are also not snapshot-pinned. Prices are keyed to the TARGET (priceIn/priceOut per TargetModelId), so an alias that starts serving a differently-priced successor mis-costs every call until an env override lands; tasks/lessons.md ('A price change is a cost-cap change') documents this exact drift class.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/providers/config.ts; cost-cap accuracy and behavior stability for deepseek_v4, mistral_large_3, qwen3_7_max
- **proposed_fix:** No default change without owner sign-off (D9 makes env the pin mechanism); minimum action is a runbook note listing the three floating aliases and the MODEL_*/PRICE_* overrides to set when the upstream alias moves.
- **verification:** docs/runbooks/ contains the alias-pinning note naming MODEL_DEEPSEEK, MODEL_MISTRAL, MODEL_QWEN and their PRICE_* pairs
- **disposition:** partially-resolved — Stage 2 W2: deepseek pinned to deepseek-v4-pro (published rates 0.435/0.87) and qwen to qwen3.7-max, both verbatim from vendor model pages 2026-08-01; mistral-large-latest DELIBERATELY floats (no published exact id — inferring one risks the invented-model-string 404 class); pin lever + procedure in docs/runbooks/providers.md

### PRV-008 — Kimi K3, MiniMax M3, and GLM-5.2 default prices are self-declared carryovers from prior model series, pending published rates

- severity `S3` · confidence `likely` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/lib/providers/config.ts:102-104 'M3 launch rates match the M2-series list pricing', :118-120 'K3 launch rates match the K2-series list pricing', :146-148 'GLM-5.2 list rates are unpublished at launch — defaults are the GLM-5 reference rates'. Not internally inconsistent (each is documented and env-overridable), but three of sixteen cost-cap price rows are admitted placeholders; gemini_3_6_flash 1.5/7.5 was verified as a deliberate reprice (commit 16dadbd 'relabel Gemini target to Flash and reprice', lessons.md '$1.20 -> $7.50').
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/providers/config.ts; daily cost cap accuracy for kimi_k3, minimax_m3, glm_5_2
- **proposed_fix:** When each vendor publishes list rates, update the numEnv defaults (or set PRICE_* in Vercel) and add the 'check the deployed overrides' changelog line lessons.md prescribes; no change until rates are confirmed.
- **verification:** Compare config.ts defaults against the vendors' published price pages at update time; unit cost via computeCost matches the published per-1M rates
- **disposition:** still-open (deferred by adjudication) — provisional rows marked in config.ts and docs/runbooks/providers.md names the PRICE_* levers; update when Moonshot/MiniMax/Z.ai publish rates

### PRV-009 — OpenAI enhance path caps completion at a flat 16k with no per-effort headroom, unlike the Anthropic path which doubles the ceiling for xhigh/max for the same reasons-bill-as-output mechanism

- severity `S3` · confidence `speculative` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/lib/providers/openai.ts:37 max_completion_tokens: 16_000 regardless of reasoning_effort; src/lib/providers/anthropic.ts:20-35 raises 32k->64k for xhigh/max explicitly because reasoning bills against the ceiling and a tight cap 'truncat[ed] envelopes'; src/lib/providers/vision.ts:298-301 documents the same OpenAI reasoning-burn mechanism ('a tight cap can be consumed before any JSON is emitted'). A gpt_5_6 run at reasoning_effort high that spends most of 16k on reasoning would surface as the adapter's 'hit its length limit' error (adapter.ts:160-163). No observed failure; the codebase's own two precedents are the basis.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/providers/openai.ts; gpt_5_6_sol/luna/terra at high reasoning effort on large inputs
- **proposed_fix:** Mirror the Anthropic pattern: raise max_completion_tokens for reasoning_effort 'high' (e.g. 32_000), keeping the cost cap and maxDuration as the true bounds.
- **verification:** Unit test on the OpenAI request builder (extract one like buildAnthropicParams/buildCompatBody) asserting the per-effort ceiling; watch prod logs for the 'hit its length limit' message on gpt targets
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: max_completion_tokens 32k at reasoning_effort high (16k otherwise), mirroring the Anthropic per-effort headroom

## Track MED

> MED track is architecturally sound but its server-side enforcement story has one real hole and several edge seams. Verified PASS: /api/media is a properly guarded model route — auth before body parse, in-memory burst limit plus atomic spend_reserve admission (rate + cap + hold under one advisory lock), one hold covering the cross-provider retry, release on every failure path, and settlement with mode 'extract' at the analyzing model's real rates (usage_events.mode is unconstrained text, so 'extract' lands; provider-reported image tokens including Gemini thoughtsTokenCount are billed, route.ts:185-201). Media-bearing enhance calls carry only bounded TEXT context blocks (max 4 x 2000 chars, server-validated at enhance/route.ts:179-194) metered as ordinary input tokens — images never ride the enhance call, so no separate multimodal metering is needed and none is missing. RLS coverage is complete on media_assets (4 owner policies), media_reserve is SECURITY INVOKER with anon revoked, usage_reservations is default-deny, storage media policies are owner-folder-scoped with no UPDATE policy (overwrite denied, matching upsert:false), and account deletion paginates and purges the media folder before deleteUser. The reserve-upload-ready ladder handles every client-visible failure direction and pending rows stay visible/deletable by design (the cited cleanup mechanism is manual removal via MediaManager; no automated sweep exists anywhere). Memory profile is bounded: whole-image base64 buffering at ~4-5 string copies, worst ~18-23 MB per request under Vercel's 4.5 MB body cap. Headline defects: (S1) the 50 MB quota is bypassable — the storage INSERT policy is not tied to a reservation and declared size_bytes is never reconciled with actual object size; (S2) bucket 25 MB vs advertised/reserved 50 MB means the 25-50 MB band always fails post-reservation; (S2) client wildcard MIME vs the bucket's 11-type allowlist strands HEIC/3gpp/x-m4a stored uploads; (S2) failed vision calls still bill nothing (DISC-06 carry-forward); (S2) upload-failure cleanup can strand an invisible storage object. Route-handler behavior is entirely untested despite 95 green tests over the pure layers.
>
| id | sev | conf | fix class | design | title |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MED-001 | S2 | certain | MANUAL-APPROVE | none | The 50 MB media quota is server-enforced only on the honor path: storage.objects INSERT policy lets any authenticated user upload directly to media/{uid}/ with no reservation, and reserved size_bytes is never reconciled against actual object size |
| MED-002 | S2 | certain | NEEDS-RULING | none | Server size limits disagree: bucket file_size_limit is 25 MB while media_reserve, admitFiles, and all user-facing copy enforce/state 50 MB, so any 25-50 MB file passes reservation and always fails at upload with a raw storage error |
| MED-003 | S2 | certain | AUTO-REVIEW | none | MIME acceptance diverges client vs server: client admits any image/*, video/*, audio/* but the bucket allows exactly 11 types, so common formats (image/heic, video/3gpp, audio/x-m4a, image/avif, image/svg+xml, audio/flac) reserve quota then fail upload with a raw storage error |
| MED-004 | S2 | certain | AUTO-REVIEW | none | Failed vision calls are never billed: /api/media releases the spend hold and returns on any provider failure, and the failed first leg of the cross-provider retry is also unaccounted (prior DISC-06, still open) |
| MED-005 | S2 | likely | AUTO-REVIEW | none | Upload-failure cleanup deletes the DB row without attempting object removal, so an upload that committed server-side while the client saw an error leaves an invisible orphaned storage object cleaned only by account deletion; pending rows themselves never expire automatically |
| MED-006 | S3 | certain | AUTO-REVIEW | none | /api/media validates intent with the 'in' operator, which accepts Object.prototype keys ('toString', 'constructor') and silently falls back to default analysis while echoing the bogus intent back to the client |
| MED-007 | S3 | certain | AUTO-SAFE | none | docs/runbooks/media.md still documents the audio generation spec as 'Tempo / Timbre / Mood / Duration' though tempo/timbre were removed as dead schema and audioSpec emits only Mood and Duration |
| MED-008 | S3 | certain | AUTO-SAFE | none | No test exercises the /api/media route handler: auth gate, size guard, target/intent validation, reserve-release-on-failure, settle wiring, and the fallbackFrom response contract are all unpinned |
| MED-009 | S3 | likely | AUTO-SAFE | none | MAX_IMAGE_BYTES (5 MB decoded) is unreachable in production: Vercel's 4.5 MB serverless request-body limit rejects the JSON envelope first, making the effective decoded ceiling ~3.37 MB and the in-route 413 dead code on the shipped platform |

### MED-001 — The 50 MB media quota is server-enforced only on the honor path: storage.objects INSERT policy lets any authenticated user upload directly to media/{uid}/ with no reservation, and reserved size_bytes is never reconciled against actual object size

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `MEDIA-01`
- **evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260613073450_p5_media_assets.sql:49-52 — media_obj_insert_own checks only bucket_id='media' and folder=auth.uid(); nothing requires a matching media_assets row. supabase/migrations/20260727091741_media_roles_and_reservation.sql:19-20 claims 'The browser must reserve before uploading' but no mechanism enforces 'must'. Quota is computed as sum(size_bytes) over media_assets rows only (same migration:65-68), and size_bytes is the CLIENT-declared file.size (src/components/media/AttachmentTray.tsx:85 passes input.sizeBytes). Verified absent: no trigger, cron, or reconciliation compares media_assets.size_bytes to storage.objects metadata (grep of supabase/migrations/*.sql for trigger/cron/metadata — only profiles/prompts triggers exist). Two bypasses: (a) direct storage API upload with the session JWT — unlimited objects at up to 26214400 bytes each (bucket file_size_limit), zero quota rows; (b) media_reserve with p_size_bytes=1 then upload 25 MB — quota charged 1 byte.
- **blast_radius:** supabase/migrations/20260613073450_p5_media_assets.sql (storage policy), 20260727091741 (media_reserve), src/lib/media/pipeline.ts, src/components/media/AttachmentTray.tsx; cost/abuse exposure on the Supabase storage bill; quota meter in MediaManager/AttachmentTray can be arbitrarily wrong
- **proposed_fix:** New migration: tighten media_obj_insert_own to require exists(select 1 from public.media_assets where storage_path = name and user_id = auth.uid() and status = 'pending'), and add a reconciliation step (e.g. flip-to-ready RPC that reads storage.objects metadata->>'size' and corrects size_bytes, failing the row on gross mismatch). Deploy-order: policy change must land after the client that always reserves first (it already does).
- **verification:** Against a test project: authenticated storage upload to media/{uid}/x.png without a media_reserve call must be rejected; reserve 1 byte then upload a 1 MB object must fail or correct size_bytes. Locally: assert the new policy SQL text in tests/unit/migrations.test.ts.
- **adversarial verdict:** DOWNGRADED — Mechanics fully CONFIRMED in code. (a) media_obj_insert_own (20260613073450:49-52) gates only bucket_id='media' + folder=auth.uid(); no media_assets correlation, so a signed-in user can supabase.storage.from('media').upload() directly (the exact wrapper at AttachmentTray.tsx:96-100) with zero reservation — uncharged objects up to the 26214400-byte bucket limit, invisible to the meter which sums media_assets.size_bytes only (AttachmentTray.tsx:182-183, MediaManager.tsx:77). (b) media_reserve (20260727091741:57,68,80) stores the client-declared p_size_bytes = file.size (AttachmentTray.tsx:85,258,290) verbatim; reserve 1 byte then upload 25 MB. No reconciliation: grep of all migrations shows triggers only on prompts/profiles, no pg_cron, no storage.objects metadata comparison; /api/media is analysis-only and never mediates uploads. Not a PR-ruling disagreement. DOWNGRADE rationale: the exploit is authenticated-only and self-scoped (folder must equal auth.uid()) — no cross-tenant exposure, no auth bypass, no secret leak, and storage.objects RLS is present (just permissive-by-owner). invariant_ref is empty so 'invariant violation => min S1' does not attach, and CLAUDE.md §6's cost-cap guardrail is scoped to model routes, not the storage bucket. This is a real but bounded cost/abuse defect (storage-bill inflation by a valid account), which is S2, not S1.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2 (migration 20260801200000, applied): media_obj_insert_own requires a matching pending reservation, and the ready-flip is the media_commit RPC which reconciles size_bytes against storage metadata (pipeline commit dep; pinned by media-pipeline.test.ts)

### MED-002 — Server size limits disagree: bucket file_size_limit is 25 MB while media_reserve, admitFiles, and all user-facing copy enforce/state 50 MB, so any 25-50 MB file passes reservation and always fails at upload with a raw storage error

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `NEEDS-RULING` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `MEDIA-01`
- **evidence:** supabase/migrations/20260613073450_p5_media_assets.sql:36 — file_size_limit 26214400 with comment '-- 25 MB'. supabase/migrations/20260727091741_media_roles_and_reservation.sql:49,57 — v_quota 52428800; invalid_size raised only when p_size_bytes > 50 MB. src/lib/media/pipeline.ts:88 — client message 'That file is too large to store (50 MB limit)'. src/lib/media/formatters.ts:104 — MEDIA_QUOTA_BYTES = 50 MB. A 30 MB mp4: admitFiles passes (under 50 MB quota), media_reserve inserts a pending row, storage upload 413s at the bucket limit, pipeline deletes the row and surfaces the raw storage message (pipeline.ts:96-109). docs/runbooks/media.md:49 also documents 50 MB.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/media/pipeline.ts, src/lib/media/queue.ts, supabase/migrations (bucket config), docs/runbooks/media.md; every stored upload in the 25-50 MB band fails after two round trips with an unexplained error
- **proposed_fix:** Rule which limit is authoritative: either raise the bucket file_size_limit to 52428800 (one-line migration updating storage.buckets) or add a 25 MB per-file client check in admitFiles plus an invalid_size guard at 25 MB in media_reserve, and correct the copy. Do not ship both limits disagreeing.
- **verification:** After the ruling: unit test in tests/unit/media-queue.test.ts rejecting (or admitting) a 30 MB file per the chosen limit; manual: attach a 30 MB mp4 and confirm it either stores or is rejected pre-upload with the correct message.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2 (ruling Q3=a): bucket file_size_limit raised to 52428800, matching media_reserve, admitFiles, and every copy surface

### MED-003 — MIME acceptance diverges client vs server: client admits any image/*, video/*, audio/* but the bucket allows exactly 11 types, so common formats (image/heic, video/3gpp, audio/x-m4a, image/avif, image/svg+xml, audio/flac) reserve quota then fail upload with a raw storage error

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** Client side: src/components/media/AttachmentTray.tsx:612 accept="image/*,video/*,audio/*"; src/lib/media/ondevice.ts:35-40 kindForMime admits any image/video/audio prefix; media_reserve validates no MIME at all (20260727091741:81 stores left(p_mime_type,100)). Server side: supabase/migrations/20260613073450_p5_media_assets.sql:37-41 allowed_mime_types = image/{png,jpeg,webp,gif}, video/{mp4,webm,quicktime}, audio/{mpeg,wav,ogg,mp4} only. Exact diff: everything matching the client wildcards but outside those 11 types is client-admitted, reservation-approved, bucket-rejected (the bucket list is a strict subset, so no type is server-accepted but client-rejected). On this iOS-first PWA (CLAUDE.md §1), HEIC via the Files picker is a mainstream case; the same file WORKS when 'Originals not kept' (ephemeral path never touches the bucket and captureFrameDataUrl transcodes to JPEG, ondevice.ts:162) and FAILS when stored — inconsistent by storage preference. /api/media itself accepts any image/* data URL (src/app/api/media/route.ts:108), wider than the bucket, but stores nothing.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/media/ondevice.ts (kindForMime), src/lib/media/queue.ts (admitFiles), src/components/media/AttachmentTray.tsx; stored-upload failures with raw error text for a set of common capture formats
- **proposed_fix:** Minimum change: mirror the bucket allowlist in a shared constant consumed by admitFiles (reject unsupported subtypes up front with the existing 'Unsupported file type' reason) and in the input accept attribute. Alternative worth a product call: widen the bucket list (HEIC/AVIF/3gpp/x-m4a) or transcode stored images to JPEG client-side the way the analysis path already does.
- **verification:** npx vitest run tests/unit/media-queue.test.ts after adding a case: admitFiles rejects {type:'image/heic'} while admitting {type:'image/jpeg'}; assert the constant equals the migration's allowed_mime_types list.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: MEDIA_ALLOWED_MIME mirrors the bucket's 11 types; kindForMime is allowlist-exact and the file input accepts the explicit list (which also makes iOS transcode HEIC at the picker)

### MED-004 — Failed vision calls are never billed: /api/media releases the spend hold and returns on any provider failure, and the failed first leg of the cross-provider retry is also unaccounted (prior DISC-06, still open)

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `DISC-06`
- **evidence:** src/app/api/media/route.ts:175-183 — the catch releases the reservation (releaseSpend) and returns visionError(e); no settleSpend, so tokens a provider consumed before erroring (e.g. a 429/400 after ingesting the image, or a mid-response failure) never reach usage_events. route.ts:161-174 — when the first attempt fails config-shaped and the fallback succeeds, only the fallback's extracted.tokenIn/tokenOut are settled (route.ts:190-197); the first attempt's consumption is dropped. /api/enhance takes the opposite position and settles usage in its stream error paths. Prior ledger docs/audits/VIZION-enhancement-ledger.json DISC-06 ('Media route never bills a failed vision call', decision 'P2 — planned next cycle') describes exactly this; the current code has restructured around reservations but the billing gap is unchanged.
- **blast_radius:** src/app/api/media/route.ts; the daily cost cap undercounts on failing/flaky providers — repeated failed analyses spend on server keys without ledger visibility
- **proposed_fix:** Where the thrown error carries usage (extend describeImage to attach tokenIn/tokenOut when the provider reported them before failing), settle instead of release; at minimum settle the failed first attempt's tokens when the fallback path is taken, mirroring the enhance route's charge-on-abort stance.
- **verification:** Unit test on the route with a mocked describeImage that throws after reporting usage: assert settleSpend (not releaseSpend) is called with the failed attempt's tokens.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: ProviderError carries provider-reported usage; describeGoogle attaches it; /api/media settles a failed leg that reported usage (per-leg rates) and sums the failed first leg into a fallback run's settle; media-route.test.ts pins both paths

### MED-005 — Upload-failure cleanup deletes the DB row without attempting object removal, so an upload that committed server-side while the client saw an error leaves an invisible orphaned storage object cleaned only by account deletion; pending rows themselves never expire automatically

- severity `S2` · confidence `likely` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `MEDIA-01`
- **evidence:** src/lib/media/pipeline.ts:96-110 — catch on uploadObject runs deleteRow (or setStatus 'failed') but never calls deps.removeObject(reserved.storagePath), despite the file header (pipeline.ts:10-12) promising 'never an invisible orphaned storage object'. If the Supabase storage write committed but the response was lost (timeout/connection reset), the object exists with no media_assets row: not counted in quota (AttachmentTray.tsx:182-183 and MediaManager.tsx:77 sum rows only), not listed (MediaManager lists media_assets), unreachable until src/app/auth/delete-account/route.ts:41-50 sweeps the whole folder. Cleanup mechanism for the OTHER direction is present and deliberate: never-readied 'pending' rows stay visible and manually deletable (MediaManager.tsx:170-174 'Incomplete upload — safe to remove'); confirmed no automatic sweeper exists anywhere (no cron/trigger in supabase/migrations, no server job).
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/media/pipeline.ts; storage-cost hygiene per affected user; quota meter undercounts actual stored bytes
- **proposed_fix:** In storeAttachment's upload-failure catch, best-effort deps.removeObject(reserved.storagePath).catch(() => {}) before the row delete — a not-found result is already modeled ({ notFound: true }), so the normal failure case is a cheap no-op.
- **verification:** npx vitest run tests/unit/media-pipeline.test.ts after adding a case: uploadObject throws, assert removeObject was called with the reserved path before deleteRow.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: storeAttachment best-effort removeObject before the row delete on upload failure; pinned by media-pipeline.test.ts

### MED-006 — /api/media validates intent with the 'in' operator, which accepts Object.prototype keys ('toString', 'constructor') and silently falls back to default analysis while echoing the bogus intent back to the client

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/app/api/media/route.ts:101 — `!(intent in INTENTS)`; INTENTS (route.ts:33-38) is a plain object literal, so 'toString' in INTENTS === true. INTENTS['toString'] is the inherited function; intentSpec.system/expect are undefined, describeImage then applies its defaults (src/lib/providers/vision.ts:275-281), and the response returns intent: 'toString' (route.ts:224-231). No crash and no privilege issue, but the input validation is porous and the echoed intent violates the response's own type story.
- **blast_radius:** src/app/api/media/route.ts only; direct-API callers get an inconsistent response shape
- **proposed_fix:** Replace the check with !Object.hasOwn(INTENTS, intent) (or Object.keys(INTENTS).includes(intent)).
- **verification:** Unit test: POST body {intent:'toString'} returns 400 'Unknown analysis intent.'
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: Object.hasOwn intent validation; prototype keys 400 (media-route.test.ts)

### MED-007 — docs/runbooks/media.md still documents the audio generation spec as 'Tempo / Timbre / Mood / Duration' though tempo/timbre were removed as dead schema and audioSpec emits only Mood and Duration

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-SAFE` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `PROD-07`
- **evidence:** docs/runbooks/media.md:95 — 'Audio — structured spec (`Tempo / Timbre / Mood / Duration`)'. src/lib/media/formatters.ts:88-99 — audioSpec emits base + Mood + Duration only, with a comment explaining the honesty fix; src/lib/media/types.ts:4-6 records the removal ('the old tempo/timbre were dead schema'); tests/unit/media.test.ts:63 asserts 'an honest audio spec (metadata only — no semantic tempo/timbre)'. The runbook contradicts code, tests, and the 2026-07 honest-capability decision it postdates.
- **blast_radius:** docs/runbooks/media.md only
- **proposed_fix:** Edit line 95 to '(`Mood / Duration` — file metadata only, no semantic tempo/timbre pass)'.
- **verification:** grep -n 'Tempo / Timbre' docs/runbooks/media.md returns nothing.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W5: docs/runbooks/media.md audio spec now reads 'Mood / Duration' (file metadata only); the dead tempo/timbre fields are gone from the doc as they are from audioSpec

### MED-008 — No test exercises the /api/media route handler: auth gate, size guard, target/intent validation, reserve-release-on-failure, settle wiring, and the fallbackFrom response contract are all unpinned

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-SAFE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** tests/unit contains 9 media/vision suites (95 tests, all pass via `npx vitest run tests/unit/media*.test.ts tests/unit/vision-fallback.test.ts tests/unit/attachment-roles.test.ts`) covering the pure layers thoroughly: pipeline ladder incl. every failure direction (media-pipeline.test.ts:32-137), admitFiles quota accumulation (media-queue.test.ts:52-67), parsers/formatters/highlight/preview-signing/fallback selection. Nothing imports or invokes src/app/api/media/route.ts (grep 'api/media' in tests matches only fetch-mocks in component tests); by contrast delete-account has a dedicated route test (tests/unit/delete-account-route.test.ts), proving the pattern is feasible here. Untested consequences already bit this route once (DISC-06). Also unpinned: the client-accepts vs bucket-allows MIME/size alignment (findings 2 and 3 had no failing test to catch them).
- **blast_radius:** tests/unit; regression risk concentrated on the one media path that spends money
- **proposed_fix:** Add tests/unit/media-route.test.ts mocking createClient/describeImage/spend helpers: 401 unauthenticated; 400 bad JSON/missing dataUrl/unknown target/unknown intent; 413 oversized base64; releaseSpend called exactly once on provider throw; settleSpend called with mode 'extract' and the fallback target after a config-shaped first failure; fallbackFrom present only when redirected.
- **verification:** npx vitest run tests/unit/media-route.test.ts green; coverage report shows src/app/api/media/route.ts exercised.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: tests/unit/media-route.test.ts covers auth gate, validation, reserve/settle/release ladder, estimated marking, failed-leg billing, fallback contract, and the pre-hold 503

### MED-009 — MAX_IMAGE_BYTES (5 MB decoded) is unreachable in production: Vercel's 4.5 MB serverless request-body limit rejects the JSON envelope first, making the effective decoded ceiling ~3.37 MB and the in-route 413 dead code on the shipped platform

- severity `S3` · confidence `likely` · fix_class `AUTO-SAFE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/app/api/media/route.ts:27 MAX_IMAGE_BYTES = 5*1024*1024, checked at route.ts:112 after request.json(). A 5 MB decoded image is ~6.99 MB of base64 (5*4/3), inside a JSON body larger still; Vercel serverless functions cap request bodies at 4.5 MB (CLAUDE.md §7: Vercel primary, Node runtime), so the platform 413s before the handler runs. Effective max decoded image = 4.5 MB * 0.75 = ~3.375 MB. Memory profile is otherwise bounded and acceptable: worst case ~4-5 in-memory copies of the base64 string (raw body, parsed JSON field, regex capture at src/lib/media/extract.ts:85-87, provider payload) = ~18-23 MB per request at the platform cap; in practice captureFrameDataUrl sends a max-1024px JPEG at q0.85 (src/lib/media/ondevice.ts:120-168), typically well under 1 MB. No streaming or disk buffering exists or is needed.
- **blast_radius:** src/app/api/media/route.ts; only self-hosted deployments without a body cap ever hit the in-route guard
- **proposed_fix:** Comment-only: note at MAX_IMAGE_BYTES that the guard is a backstop for non-Vercel hosts and that the platform body cap (4.5 MB) binds first in production. Optionally lower the constant to match reality.
- **verification:** Comment change only — npm run lint && npm run typecheck stay green; no runtime path changes.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: MAX_IMAGE_BYTES documented as a non-Vercel backstop (platform 4.5 MB body cap binds first)

## Track LIB

> Track LIB is structurally strong: the 2026-07-30 library_integrity migration genuinely fixes the prior audit's DB-02 class (save and add-version are single SECURITY INVOKER RPCs with a FOR UPDATE parent lock; a trigger makes prompts.current_ver provably same-prompt; restore validates ownership app-side AND fails closed DB-side), RLS is present with owner-only policies on all five library tables from creation (prompts, prompt_versions select/insert-only for immutability, activity_events, collections, drafts) and migrations.test.ts enforces 'RLS on every table it creates', the diff engine's lossless-reconstruction and accept/reject invariants are unit-pinned and bounded (DIFF-01 resolved), the content hash byte-matches the SQL backfill via a live-DB fixture, drafts get correct optimistic concurrency with an exhaustive test suite, and all 63 targeted unit tests pass. Version chains are trees by design (restore-then-add forks are benign; history renders chronologically) and hard-delete cascade order is sound. Eleven findings survive verification: four S2 — LibraryBrowser keeps stale accumulated pages and a stale cursor across router.refresh() (duplicate/skipped rows at page seams; DraftsList fixed and tested the identical bug, LibraryBrowser didn't), the duplicate-save race persists cross-tab and cross-device because dedupe is check-then-insert with no DB uniqueness and the outbox flush guard is per-tab (LIB-02 carry-forward, acknowledged in the code's own comment), there is no pruning or growth limit anywhere (unbounded prompts/versions/drafts/activity_events, no text-length CHECKs, only Next's default 1MB action body), and prompt_versions.parent_ver lacks the same-prompt/cycle guard that current_ver received, leaving the chain corruptible by direct PostgREST inserts the RLS policy allows. Seven S3s cover raw cursor interpolation into PostgREST or() grammar (RLS-confined), unordered facet sampling at limit(1000), UI-only enforcement of archived-only hard delete plus mutable soft-deleted prompts, forever-retrying outbox poison items and GenerateSheet ignoring the duplicate contract, collection-delete bumping updated_at on released prompts via the trigger, the hash excluding target_model, and the drafts-vs-library test-coverage asymmetry that let the S2 seam bug persist.
>
| id | sev | conf | fix class | design | title |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| LIB-001 | S2 | certain | MANUAL-APPROVE | none | No pruning or per-user growth limits anywhere in the library: prompts, versions per prompt, drafts count, and activity_events grow without bound, and version bodies have no length cap |
| LIB-002 | S2 | certain | MANUAL-APPROVE | none | prompt_versions.parent_ver has no same-prompt or acyclicity guard — the version chain can be corrupted (cross-prompt, cross-user, or self-referential parent) by a direct PostgREST insert the RLS insert policy allows |
| LIB-003 | S2 | likely | AUTO-REVIEW | none | LibraryBrowser keeps stale accumulated pages and a stale keyset cursor across router.refresh(), producing duplicate rows and permanently skipped rows at page seams |
| LIB-004 | S2 | likely | MANUAL-APPROVE | none | Duplicate-save race survives the content-hash fix: check-then-insert with no DB uniqueness and no cross-tab flush mutex can mint two identical prompt cards |
| LIB-005 | S3 | certain | AUTO-REVIEW | none | queryLibraryFacets derives model counts, tags, and collection counts from an UNORDERED .limit(1000) slice — beyond 1000 prompts the facet numbers come from an arbitrary subset |
| LIB-006 | S3 | certain | AUTO-REVIEW | none | Delete semantics enforced only in the UI: hard delete is not restricted to archived prompts server-side, and soft-deleted prompts remain fully readable and mutable via the detail route |
| LIB-007 | S3 | certain | AUTO-REVIEW | none | Outbox poison items retry forever and are never surfaced: a queued save whose payload later fails validation (e.g. its target was removed from the roster) can never drain, and the user's 'Queued — syncs when online' promise is silently broken |
| LIB-008 | S3 | certain | AUTO-SAFE | none | Coverage asymmetry: queryLibraryPage's PostgREST emission and LibraryBrowser's page-seam behavior are untested, while the drafts twins of both are |
| LIB-009 | S3 | likely | MANUAL-APPROVE | none | Deleting a collection silently reorders the library: ON DELETE SET NULL fires the prompts updated_at trigger, bumping every released prompt to the top of the Recent sort |
| LIB-010 | S3 | likely | NEEDS-RULING | none | content_hash excludes target_model, so identical input/output/mode saved for a DIFFERENT destination model is flagged as a duplicate and 'Save as new version' files it under the wrong target |

### LIB-001 — No pruning or per-user growth limits anywhere in the library: prompts, versions per prompt, drafts count, and activity_events grow without bound, and version bodies have no length cap

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** grep across src/ and supabase/ for prune/retention/max_versions/quota finds only the media 50MB quota (src/lib/media/formatters.ts:104) — nothing for the library. savePromptAction/addVersionAction validate() checks only non-empty + enum membership (src/lib/library/actions.ts:61-66); prompt_versions.input_text/output_text have no CHECK (20260613072034_p4_library_versioning.sql:29-31); the only ceiling is Next's default 1MB server-action body (next.config.ts declares no serverActions.bodySizeLimit — grep returns nothing). Every save inserts 2 activity_events rows (library_integrity.sql:109-111, 164-166) yet only the latest 20 are ever read (src/app/(app)/library/page.tsx:88-92) and no delete policy or job exists. drafts bounds one row at 100k chars (20260730012046_drafts.sql:38) but row count per user is unbounded. Contrast: media_assets got an atomic 50MB reservation quota (20260727091741_media_roles_and_reservation.sql), so the pattern exists in-repo.
- **blast_radius:** supabase tables prompts, prompt_versions, drafts, activity_events; hosted-project storage cost and queryLibraryFacets accuracy (see separate finding)
- **proposed_fix:** Owner decision needed on limits: CHECK char_length bounds on prompt_versions text columns and drafts row count per user, an activity_events retention trim (e.g. keep latest N per user, deleted in the save RPCs or a scheduled job), and optionally a per-prompt version ceiling with oldest-non-current pruning.
- **verification:** After migration: insert past each limit and assert the constraint/RPC rejects or trims; select count(*) from activity_events for a heavy user stays bounded.
- **disposition:** pending

### LIB-002 — prompt_versions.parent_ver has no same-prompt or acyclicity guard — the version chain can be corrupted (cross-prompt, cross-user, or self-referential parent) by a direct PostgREST insert the RLS insert policy allows

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `DB-02`
- **evidence:** 20260613072034_p4_library_versioning.sql:28 (`parent_ver uuid references public.prompt_versions (id)` — FK only, no same-prompt predicate, no cycle guard) and :86-92 (prompt_versions_insert_own checks only that prompt_id's parent prompt is owned; parent_ver is unconstrained). FK checks do not consult RLS, so parent_ver can name another USER'S version id (an existence oracle: FK violation vs success distinguishes real uuids), and a client-supplied id can self-parent. 20260730234204_library_integrity.sql guards only prompts.current_ver (trigger :39-61) — its own rationale ('the application predicate can be bypassed by any direct PostgREST write; the trigger cannot', tests/unit/library-integrity.test.ts:103-105) applies verbatim to the sibling pointer it left unguarded. Consumer impact: PromptDetail seeds its default compare pair from current?.parent_ver (src/app/(app)/library/[id]/page.tsx:40-42, PromptDetail.tsx:112); a cross-prompt parent makes getVersionBodyAction (actions.ts:320-325, .eq prompt_id) return 'Version not found' and the compare errors. In-app writers are safe: library_add_version derives parent from the locked row's current_ver (library_integrity.sql:142-155). Fork-by-restore (two children of one parent after restore-then-add) is intentional tree semantics, not a defect — history renders chronologically.
- **blast_radius:** supabase/migrations (new trigger); prompt_versions writes; PromptDetail default-compare path
- **proposed_fix:** Extend enforce-style trigger to prompt_versions: before insert, if new.parent_ver is not null require exists(select 1 from prompt_versions v where v.id = new.parent_ver and v.prompt_id = new.prompt_id) and new.parent_ver <> new.id; raise 'parent_not_of_prompt' otherwise.
- **verification:** SQL test on a branch DB: insert a version with parent_ver from a different prompt and with parent_ver = its own supplied id — both must raise; library_add_version continues to succeed.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2 (migration 20260801200000, applied): enforce_version_parent trigger — parent_ver must be a version of the same prompt and never self

### LIB-003 — LibraryBrowser keeps stale accumulated pages and a stale keyset cursor across router.refresh(), producing duplicate rows and permanently skipped rows at page seams

- severity `S2` · confidence `likely` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/components/library/LibraryBrowser.tsx:79-80 (`extraCards` state; `useState(nextCursor)` never re-initializes on refreshed props), :85 (`const cards = [...initialCards, ...extraCards]`, no dedupe by id), :102/:119/:312/:326 (swipeDelete/swipeFavorite/CardActionsSheet all call router.refresh() without resetting extraCards or cursor), :239 (key={p.id} — a card present in both refreshed page 1 and stale extraCards renders twice with a duplicate React key). Every prompt update bumps updated_at via prompts_set_updated_at (supabase/migrations/20260613072034_p4_library_versioning.sql:61-63), so favoriting a card that sits in extraCards moves it into the refreshed page 1 while the stale copy remains below. DraftsList documents and fixes exactly this bug class in the same repo (src/components/library/DraftsList.tsx:84-120, 198-203) and tests it (tests/unit/drafts-list.test.tsx:362 'pages from the CURRENT first-page boundary, not one captured at mount'); LibraryBrowser has neither the fix nor a test. The page-level key={libraryHref(filter)} (src/app/(app)/library/page.tsx:101) resets state only on filter change, not on refresh.
- **blast_radius:** src/components/library/LibraryBrowser.tsx; /library route for any user who taps Load more then mutates a card (favorite, archive, delete, rename, move)
- **proposed_fix:** Port DraftsList's pattern: derive the effective cursor from the prop until paged (undefined sentinel), and reset extraCards + pagedCursor before every router.refresh() triggered by a mutation; optionally dedupe cards by id at concat.
- **verification:** Unit test mirroring tests/unit/drafts-list.test.tsx:362 for LibraryBrowser: load page 2, mock a favorite toggle + refreshed initialCards containing a page-2 card, assert no duplicate card ids and that the next Load more uses the refreshed boundary.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: LibraryBrowser ports the DraftsList pattern (pagedCursor sentinel, refreshAfterMutation funnel incl. CardActionsSheet via onMutated, id-dedupe at concat, refresh in its own transition); seam tests in library-card.test.tsx

### LIB-004 — Duplicate-save race survives the content-hash fix: check-then-insert with no DB uniqueness and no cross-tab flush mutex can mint two identical prompt cards

- severity `S2` · confidence `likely` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `LIB-02`
- **evidence:** src/lib/library/actions.ts:90-103 (duplicate lookup) then :117 (rpc library_save_prompt) — two concurrent identical saves both pass the check before either inserts; idx_prompt_versions_hash is a plain, non-unique index (supabase/migrations/20260727093842_library_organization.sql:54-55) and library_save_prompt (20260730234204_library_integrity.sql:70-114) never re-checks the hash. Concrete trigger: OutboxFlusher's re-entrancy guard is a per-tab closure variable (src/components/pwa/OutboxFlusher.tsx:31 `let flushing = false`) while the IndexedDB store is origin-shared (src/lib/pwa/outbox.ts:74 DB_NAME 'vizion-outbox'); two open tabs both receive 'online'/'visibilitychange', both read the same item via store.all(), and both replay it concurrently — the file's own comment (OutboxFlusher.tsx:28-30) states 'two concurrent flushes over the same items would duplicate saves — savePromptAction is not idempotent' but only guards within one tab. The single-attempt network-drop case IS handled (replay hits the hash check, res.duplicate treated as drained, OutboxFlusher.tsx:16).
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/library/actions.ts, src/components/pwa/OutboxFlusher.tsx, supabase/migrations (new); affects savePromptAction from TransformationDiff, GenerateSheet, and outbox replay
- **proposed_fix:** Cross-tab mutual exclusion for the flush (navigator.locks.request('vizion-outbox-flush', ...) with fallback), plus re-check the content hash inside library_save_prompt under pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(auth.uid()::text || p_content_hash)) and return the existing prompt id as a duplicate.
- **verification:** Unit test: flushOutbox invoked twice concurrently over a shared fake store with a handler that records invocations — assert the item's handler runs once. DB: two concurrent library_save_prompt calls with the same hash leave exactly one prompt.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: cross-tab Web Lock on the outbox flush (ifAvailable skip) + server-side pg_advisory_xact_lock per (owner,hash) with an under-lock duplicate re-check in library_save_prompt that converges both racers on one card (migration 20260801200000, applied)

### LIB-005 — queryLibraryFacets derives model counts, tags, and collection counts from an UNORDERED .limit(1000) slice — beyond 1000 prompts the facet numbers come from an arbitrary subset

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `LIB-04`
- **evidence:** src/lib/library/queries.ts:152-157: `.select("target_model, tags, collection_id").is("deleted_at", null).limit(1000)` with no .order() — PostgREST row order without ORDER BY is unspecified, so which 1000 rows feed the counts is arbitrary and can shift between requests. CollectionSheet renders these counts as authoritative (src/components/library/CollectionSheet.tsx:132) and the filter sheet's promise is 'models actually present ... with counts' (queries.ts:40-41). queryDraftModelFacets has the same shape (src/lib/drafts/queries.ts:147, limit 1000).
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/library/queries.ts, src/lib/drafts/queries.ts; filter sheet and Move-to-collection counts for libraries >1000 prompts
- **proposed_fix:** Add a deterministic .order (e.g. updated_at desc) so the slice is at least stable/most-recent, and document the cap; longer term move to a grouped-count RPC since PostgREST aggregates are disabled (PGRST123 per the comment at queries.ts:146-150).
- **verification:** Unit test asserting the emitted builder chain includes an order() call before limit(1000).
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: both facet queries order by updated_at desc before the 1000 cap — a stable most-recent slice

### LIB-006 — Delete semantics enforced only in the UI: hard delete is not restricted to archived prompts server-side, and soft-deleted prompts remain fully readable and mutable via the detail route

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** deletePromptAction (src/lib/library/actions.ts:457-474) deletes any owned prompt id — the 'Reserved for archived prompts' rule in its own doc comment (:457-458) is enforced only by the UI hiding the button (LibraryBrowser.tsx:409-419; tests/unit/library-card.test.tsx:94-100). Similarly, /library/[id] (src/app/(app)/library/[id]/page.tsx:24-28) selects without a deleted_at filter, and addVersionAction/restoreVersionAction/updateTagsAction never check deleted_at, so a soft-deleted (trash) prompt accepts new versions and restores while excluded from every list, facet, and the duplicate check (actions.ts:94 `.is("prompts.deleted_at", null)`). Cascade correctness itself is sound: prompts delete cascades versions (p4:27), current_ver set-null (p4:42-45), activity prompt_id set-null (p4:51), collection membership vanishes with the row; collection delete releases prompts via on delete set null (20260727141615_collections.sql:42) — PASS.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/library/actions.ts, src/app/(app)/library/[id]/page.tsx; owner-only data (no cross-tenant exposure)
- **proposed_fix:** Add `.not("archived_at", "is", null)` to deletePromptAction's WHERE; decide whether soft-deleted prompts should 404 or render a 'in trash — restore?' state on the detail route, and gate addVersion/restore on deleted_at is null.
- **verification:** Unit test: deletePromptAction against a non-archived id matches zero rows; detail route returns notFound (or trash state) for a deleted_at-set prompt.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: hard delete requires archived_at server-side; addVersion/restore gate on deleted_at null; the detail route 404s soft-deleted prompts (trash browsing lands with Q9 in W3)

### LIB-007 — Outbox poison items retry forever and are never surfaced: a queued save whose payload later fails validation (e.g. its target was removed from the roster) can never drain, and the user's 'Queued — syncs when online' promise is silently broken

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `LIB-02`
- **evidence:** src/components/pwa/OutboxFlusher.tsx:9-17: the handler returns `res.ok || Boolean(res.duplicate)` — a permanent validation rejection from savePromptAction ('Unknown target model.'/'Unknown mode.'/'Nothing to save.', src/lib/library/actions.ts:61-66) returns false forever, so flushOutbox retries the item on every 'online' and 'visibilitychange' event indefinitely (src/lib/pwa/outbox.ts:56-67 keeps failed items) with no user-visible surfacing. The roster demonstrably churns (five model-roster migrations between 2026-07-10 and 2026-07-26), so a payload queued under an old build can become permanently invalid. Related contract gap in the same save path: GenerateSheet ignores res.duplicate entirely (src/components/media/GenerateSheet.tsx:82-84 shows 'Couldn't save to the library.' for content that IS already saved) — media-track file, but it consumes the library save contract.
- **blast_radius:** src/components/pwa/OutboxFlusher.tsx, src/lib/pwa/outbox.ts, src/components/media/GenerateSheet.tsx
- **proposed_fix:** Distinguish permanent rejections (validation errors) from transient ones in the handler result: park permanently-failed items under a dead-letter kind and surface a one-time toast ('A queued save couldn't be synced — open to recover the text'); handle res.duplicate in GenerateSheet like TransformationDiff does.
- **verification:** Unit test in the flushOutbox suite (tests/unit/security.test.ts pattern): a handler reporting 'permanent' removes/parks the item after one attempt instead of leaving it for the next flush.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: poison/failed outbox items park after MAX_OUTBOX_ATTEMPTS with a one-time toast; GenerateSheet treats res.duplicate as the existing card (links it) instead of an error

### LIB-008 — Coverage asymmetry: queryLibraryPage's PostgREST emission and LibraryBrowser's page-seam behavior are untested, while the drafts twins of both are

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-SAFE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** tests/unit/drafts-queries.test.ts covers queryDraftsPage's or() grammar, escaping, quoting, PGRST205, and error propagation via a recording fake; no equivalent exists for queryLibraryPage (grep queryLibraryPage in tests/ hits only component tests: library-brand.test.tsx, library-card.test.tsx, new-prompt-fab.test.tsx — none exercise the builder). tests/unit/drafts-list.test.tsx:361-441 pins the post-refresh cursor lifecycle; no LibraryBrowser test covers Load more at all. tests/unit/library-integrity.test.ts is regex-over-source (structural, cannot catch behavioral drift in the RPCs). Diff invariants, hash byte-match, cursor round-trip, and flushOutbox account-scoping ARE well tested (tests/unit/diff.test.ts, library-hash.test.ts, library-paging.test.ts, security.test.ts:45-140) — 63/63 pass via `npx vitest run` on those five suites.
- **blast_radius:** tests/unit/ (new files); guards the two S2 pagination/dedupe findings against regression
- **proposed_fix:** Add library-queries.test.ts (fakeSupabase recorder asserting deleted_at/archived filters, cursor or() grammar per sort incl. quoted title, PAGE_SIZE+1 sentinel) and a LibraryBrowser seam test mirroring drafts-list.test.tsx:362.
- **verification:** npx vitest run tests/unit/library-queries.test.ts tests/unit/library-browser.test.tsx green.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: library-queries.test.ts (filters, sentinel, escaping, error propagation) + LibraryBrowser seam tests

### LIB-009 — Deleting a collection silently reorders the library: ON DELETE SET NULL fires the prompts updated_at trigger, bumping every released prompt to the top of the Recent sort

- severity `S3` · confidence `likely` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** collections FK: prompts.collection_id 'on delete set null' (supabase/migrations/20260727141615_collections.sql:41-42); referential SET NULL is executed as an UPDATE on prompts, and prompts_set_updated_at is BEFORE UPDATE for each row with no column list (20260613072034_p4_library_versioning.sql:61-63), so every released prompt gets updated_at = now(). The default library sort is updated_at desc (src/lib/library/queries.ts:46-53), so deleting a 20-prompt collection pushes all 20 above genuinely recent work. The ConfirmSheet promises only 'Prompts inside are kept — they just leave the collection' (CollectionSheet.tsx:211). Cannot execute against a live DB in this environment, hence 'likely' rather than certain; Postgres documents user triggers firing on referential-action updates.
- **blast_radius:** supabase trigger definition; /library Recent ordering after a collection delete
- **proposed_fix:** Scope the trigger: `create trigger prompts_set_updated_at before update on public.prompts for each row when (old.* is distinct from new.*)` is insufficient; instead exclude the pure collection release, e.g. a column-list trigger (before update of title, tags, favorite, archived_at, deleted_at, current_ver, preview, current_mode) or have set_updated_at skip when only collection_id changed.
- **verification:** Branch-DB test: delete a collection with member prompts and assert their updated_at is unchanged.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2 (migration 20260801200000, applied): prompts_set_updated_at is a column-list trigger excluding collection_id — collection release/assignment no longer counts as recency

### LIB-010 — content_hash excludes target_model, so identical input/output/mode saved for a DIFFERENT destination model is flagged as a duplicate and 'Save as new version' files it under the wrong target

- severity `S3` · confidence `likely` · fix_class `NEEDS-RULING` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `LIB-02`
- **evidence:** Hash formula is input || US || output || US || mode only (src/lib/library/hash.ts:11-15; 20260727093842_library_organization.sql:36-39). savePromptAction computes it without target (actions.ts:85) and the duplicate branch offers addVersionAction to the existing prompt (TransformationDiff.tsx:220-242), whose prompts.target_model is never updated (library_add_version touches current_ver/preview/current_mode only, library_integrity.sql:158-162) and prompt_versions carries no target column at all (p4:25-37). Byte-identical outputs across runs are documented reality for shape-preserving modes: docs/audits/VIZION-enhancement-evaluation.md ('Polish returned an already-structured prompt byte-identical'). Result: a prompt saved 'for Opus 5' absorbs a version the user produced 'for Kimi K3', and the library's target facet misattributes it.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/library/hash.ts (formula change would need a coordinated SQL backfill — MANUAL territory), or UI copy in TransformationDiff
- **proposed_fix:** Ruling needed: either (a) include target in the hash (requires new migration + backfill and loosens dedupe), or (b) keep the hash but have the duplicate card state the existing prompt's target and offer 'Save separately for <target>'. Option (b) avoids touching the pinned byte-match contract.
- **verification:** Unit test on the duplicate branch asserting the chosen behavior; if (a), re-pin the fixture in tests/unit/library-hash.test.ts against a live DB digest.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2 (ruling Q5=a): target joined the hash formula in hash.ts + SQL backfill (parent prompt's target_model for historical rows); fixture re-pinned against the live digest

## Track SW

> Track SW is structurally sound: the caching architecture is correct and the prior audit's P0 cache leak (CACHE-01) is genuinely fixed. Verified PASS evidence: (1) built public/sw.js is gitignored (.gitignore:49 '/public/sw.js') and produced by the prebuild hook (baseline build.log: '[build-sw] Precached 21 entries (320.8 KiB)'); (2) precache manifest exactly matches the glob and reality — 21 entries = 19 files in public/icons + manifest.webmanifest + offline.html, extracted from the built sw.js; hashed /_next assets are deliberately runtime-cached, not precached; (3) route classes: same-origin script/style/font/image → StaleWhileRevalidate 'vizion-shell' (80 entries / 7d expiration), navigations → NetworkOnly (never cached, but routed so setCatchHandler serves precached /offline.html on failure — proven end-to-end by tests/e2e/shell.spec.ts:282-311 on Chromium and by source-contract tests in tests/unit/library-integrity.test.ts; 29/29 targeted unit tests pass); every route, authed or not, falls back to offline.html offline, which self-recovers via its online-reload script preserving the original URL; (4) auth/API exclusion holds: the only caching route is a same-origin destination allowlist, so cross-origin Supabase /auth/v1 and storage responses can never enter Cache Storage, POST-only /api/enhance and /api/media are untouched (Workbox routes are GET-only), and the avatar <Image> is unoptimized with a cross-origin Supabase src (SettingsPanel.tsx:194-201) so no account image transits /_next/image into the shared cache; the /sign-in purge (register-sw.ts:56-64) adds defense in depth; (5) update flow: sw.js served 'no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate' with Service-Worker-Allowed (next.config.ts:218-225), registration.update() on every visibilitychange for long-lived standalone sessions, skipWaiting + clientsClaim + cleanupOutdatedCaches — on a new deploy an open client gets the new worker within one foreground cycle, and because navigations are network-only there is no stale-HTML window; cache invalidation does not need a version-tied runtime cache name since precache entries carry content-hash revisions and the SWR cache revalidates per use; (6) iOS constraints honored: no Background Sync/SyncManager anywhere in src, outbox flushes on online + visibilitychange with a re-entrancy guard, navigator.storage.persist() is double-optional-chained with the runbook-mandated caveat comment, and middleware.ts:15 excludes sw.js/manifest/offline.html/icons/splash from the auth gate — consistent with PR-1..PR-7 and docs/runbooks/ios-verification.md (Background Sync row: 'consistent — OutboxFlusher's premise stands'). Headline defects: the prior audit's other P0, SAFE-02 (truthful offline queue status), is still open — enqueueOutbox remains void-and-swallowing and TransformationDiff still reports 'Queued' unconditionally, including for online server failures, while the media sibling (GenerateSheet) already implements the correct gated shape; adjacent to it, both save surfaces enqueue with userId ?? '' which can strand a freshly queued item forever. The rest is comment drift (two files still describe the pre-CACHE-01 HTML-caching behavior; build-sw.mjs claims a phantom /enhance precache entry), dead skip-waiting plumbing, unbounded outbox retries, and one under-asserting e2e test.
>
| id | sev | conf | fix class | design | title |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SW-001 | S1 | certain | MANUAL-APPROVE | none | Offline queue status is still untruthful: enqueueOutbox swallows every failure and TransformationDiff reports 'Queued' unconditionally, including on online server errors (prior-audit SAFE-02, rated P0, still open) |
| SW-002 | S2 | likely | AUTO-REVIEW | none | enqueueOutbox is called with userId ?? '' — an item queued before the UI store hydrates the account id is stranded forever: never replayed, never removed, while the UI says 'Queued' |
| SW-003 | S3 | certain | AUTO-SAFE | none | Stale comments in register-sw.ts and sw-src.js still describe the pre-CACHE-01 behavior (visited page HTML cached in vizion-shell), contradicting the NetworkOnly navigation route in the same file |
| SW-004 | S3 | certain | AUTO-SAFE | none | build-sw.mjs header comment claims a manually injected /enhance app-shell precache entry with a build-timestamp revision; neither exists in the code or the built output |
| SW-005 | S3 | certain | AUTO-REVIEW | none | Redundant dead update-flow plumbing: sw-src.js calls self.skipWaiting() unconditionally on install, making the SKIP_WAITING message channel and register-sw's 'ask it to activate' handler dead code with a misleading comment |
| SW-006 | S3 | certain | AUTO-SAFE | none | e2e test name promises 'no-store cache policy' for /sw.js but asserts only 'no-cache', weaker than both its name and the actual header |
