### SW-001 — Offline queue status is still untruthful: enqueueOutbox swallows every failure and TransformationDiff reports 'Queued' unconditionally, including on online server errors (prior-audit SAFE-02, rated P0, still open)

- severity `S1` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `SAFE-02; PWA-01 (dependency SAFE-02)`
- **evidence:** src/lib/pwa/outbox.ts:123-140 — enqueueOutbox returns Promise<void> and its catch block swallows IndexedDB failures ('nothing more we can do offline'), so no caller can ever learn a queue write failed. src/components/diff/TransformationDiff.tsx:200-215 — both branches call setQueued(true) unconditionally after awaiting the void enqueueOutbox; the catch at :212 is NOT gated on navigator.onLine, so a genuine online savePromptAction throw (500/network) also renders the queued state. The gated sibling pattern the prior audit called correct exists at src/components/media/GenerateSheet.tsx:85-91 (catch checks navigator.onLine === false before enqueueing, else shows an error). Prior audit docs/audits/VIZION-enhancement-evaluation.md (SAFE-02 section) documented exactly this shape at P0; commit ecbd838 fixed SAFE-01 (userId scoping) but left the void signature and the ungated catch untouched. In Private Browsing or under quota pressure the IDB put rejects, the user sees 'Queued — syncs when online', and the prompt is gone — contradicting SECURITY.md's 'never the only copy' posture (CLAUDE.md section 6: server is the source of truth).
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/pwa/outbox.ts, src/components/diff/TransformationDiff.tsx, src/components/media/GenerateSheet.tsx; the save path on /enhance and the media generate sheet
- **proposed_fix:** Make enqueueOutbox return boolean (true only when store.put resolved); in TransformationDiff drive setQueued from that result and route false into the existing setSaveError path; gate the catch at TransformationDiff.tsx:212 on navigator.onLine === false to match GenerateSheet's shape so online failures report an error instead of a queue.
- **verification:** Unit test: inject an OutboxStore whose put rejects, assert enqueueOutbox resolves false; component test forcing savePromptAction to throw while navigator.onLine is true, assert the error state renders and the queued chip does not.
- **adversarial verdict:** CONFIRMED — Read all three cited files at HEAD (a4072e6, clean tree): outbox.ts:123-140 enqueueOutbox returns Promise<void> and its catch swallows all store.put failures; TransformationDiff.tsx:200-215 calls setQueued(true) unconditionally in both the offline branch and an ungated catch, so online savePromptAction throws also render "Queued"; GenerateSheet.tsx:85-92 has the correctly onLine-gated pattern, proving the divergence. Prior audit SAFE-02 (evaluation.md lines 56-63, ledger.json) rated exactly this P0; commit ecbd838 touched both files but only for SAFE-01 userId scoping — the void signature and ungated catch are unchanged. Platform rulings PR-1..PR-7 (vibration, share, WebGPU, splash, guest auth, taxonomy) do not cover this. Failure path is real: a rejecting IDB put (Private Browsing/quota) is swallowed, the UI shows "Queued — syncs when online", and the prompt persists nowhere, contradicting the "local cache never the only copy" guardrail. S1 severity holds: silent user data loss behind a false success indicator, but no cross-account/security/monetary dimension.
- **independently found by:** PRI ("Offline save status is still not truthful when the queue write itself fails: enqueueOutbox swallows IndexedDB "); PRI ("Offline queue status is still untruthful: enqueueOutbox swallows every IndexedDB failure and the save path rep")
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: enqueueOutbox returns whether the write landed; both save surfaces claim Queued only on a landed write with a real owner, and online failures report errors (GenerateSheet's gated shape everywhere)

### SW-002 — enqueueOutbox is called with userId ?? '' — an item queued before the UI store hydrates the account id is stranded forever: never replayed, never removed, while the UI says 'Queued'

- severity `S2` · confidence `likely` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `SAFE-01`
- **evidence:** src/components/diff/TransformationDiff.tsx:203,213 and src/components/media/GenerateSheet.tsx:77,87 pass `userId ?? ""` where userId comes from useUIStore (src/stores/ui.ts:85,136 — `userId: string | null`, null until ProfileHydrator's useEffect runs, src/components/ProfileHydrator.tsx:24-41). flushOutbox filters strictly `item.userId === userId` (src/lib/pwa/outbox.ts:53) and the flusher is always given a real Supabase user.id (src/app/(app)/layout.tsx:38), which can never equal "" — so an item stamped "" matches outbox.ts:41-45's 'belonging to nobody... never replayed and never removed' path despite being freshly queued this session. The trigger window is narrow (first-ever load before the hydrator effect commits, since userId persists across loads per stores/ui.ts:223), but the code path is unconditional and the failure is silent permanent data loss plus an immortal IDB row.
- **blast_radius:** src/components/diff/TransformationDiff.tsx, src/components/media/GenerateSheet.tsx; offline save on /enhance and media generate
- **proposed_fix:** Refuse to enqueue without an owner: if userId is falsy, fall through to the error path instead of enqueueing with "" — or plumb the authoritative user.id down as a prop the way the layout already does for OutboxFlusher.
- **verification:** Unit test: enqueue with userId "" into a fake store, run flushOutbox with any real id, assert flushed 0 / remaining 1 (reproduces the strand); after fix, assert enqueue is refused or the real id is stamped.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: enqueueOutbox refuses a falsy userId and callers route the refusal to the error path

### SW-003 — Stale comments in register-sw.ts and sw-src.js still describe the pre-CACHE-01 behavior (visited page HTML cached in vizion-shell), contradicting the NetworkOnly navigation route in the same file

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-SAFE` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `CACHE-01`
- **evidence:** src/lib/pwa/register-sw.ts:49-53 — 'The runtime cache that holds visited page HTML (see sw-src.js)' — false: vizion-shell holds only script/style/font/image destinations (sw-src.js:80-96) and navigations are NetworkOnly (sw-src.js:114). src/lib/pwa/sw-src.js:50-52 — 'authenticated routes the user has visited are served from the runtime stale-while-revalidate cache instead' — contradicts route 2 fourteen lines below; offline, every navigation (visited or not) serves /offline.html. These comments describe the exact behavior CACHE-01 removed and invite its reintroduction; docs/architecture.md:24-49 has the correct current description.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/pwa/register-sw.ts, src/lib/pwa/sw-src.js (comments only)
- **proposed_fix:** Rewrite both comments to match reality: vizion-shell holds same-origin static assets only; navigations are NetworkOnly and offline always falls back to /offline.html; the /sign-in purge clears cached assets, not HTML.
- **verification:** grep -n 'visited page HTML' src/lib/pwa/register-sw.ts and grep -n 'served from the runtime' src/lib/pwa/sw-src.js return nothing; npx vitest run tests/unit/library-integrity.test.ts stays green (its SW assertions target code, not these comments).
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W5: register-sw.ts and sw-src.js comments now say the runtime cache holds only same-origin static assets (SWR), never page HTML — navigations are NetworkOnly → offline.html (CACHE-01)

### SW-004 — build-sw.mjs header comment claims a manually injected /enhance app-shell precache entry with a build-timestamp revision; neither exists in the code or the built output

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-SAFE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** scripts/build-sw.mjs:15-18 — 'we precache from the static public/ directory plus a manually injected app-shell entry for /enhance. The shell revision is a build timestamp so it revalidates each deploy.' The injectManifest call at :53-58 passes only globDirectory 'public' with globPatterns [icons/**/*.png, manifest.webmanifest, offline.html] and no additionalManifestEntries; the built public/sw.js manifest contains exactly 21 entries (19 icons + manifest.webmanifest + offline.html, verified by extracting url fields) with no /enhance entry and content-hash revisions. The in-function comment at :49-52 and sw-src.js:48-53 correctly state the opposite design (no app route is safe to precache).
- **blast_radius:** scripts/build-sw.mjs (comment only)
- **proposed_fix:** Delete the phantom '/enhance entry' and 'build timestamp revision' sentences from the header comment; the accurate rationale already exists at lines 49-52.
- **verification:** grep -n 'enhance' scripts/build-sw.mjs returns nothing; node scripts/build-sw.mjs still reports 'Precached 21 entries'.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W5: build-sw.mjs header no longer claims a manually injected /enhance app-shell entry; it precaches from public/ only (icons + manifest + offline.html), matching the NetworkOnly design

### SW-005 — Redundant dead update-flow plumbing: sw-src.js calls self.skipWaiting() unconditionally on install, making the SKIP_WAITING message channel and register-sw's 'ask it to activate' handler dead code with a misleading comment

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/lib/pwa/sw-src.js:57-59 — install handler calls self.skipWaiting() unconditionally, so a new worker never enters the waiting state. The message listener at sw-src.js:68-72 and the register-sw.ts:80-92 updatefound/statechange handler that posts {type:'SKIP_WAITING'} therefore never change behavior; register-sw.ts:86-88's comment ('ask it to activate immediately so updates roll out without a reload') misdescribes the flow — activation is already immediate. Update rollout itself is sound: sw.js is served no-cache/no-store (next.config.ts:220-224, asserted by tests/e2e/shell.spec.ts:274-280) and registration.update() runs on every visibilitychange (register-sw.ts:96-100), with clientsClaim + cleanupOutdatedCaches handling takeover; NetworkOnly navigations mean no stale HTML after a deploy.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/pwa/sw-src.js, src/lib/pwa/register-sw.ts
- **proposed_fix:** Pick one activation model: either drop the unconditional install-time skipWaiting and keep the message-driven path (controlled activation), or keep unconditional skipWaiting and delete the dead message listener + postMessage handler and correct the comment. Minimum viable change is the latter (behavior-identical).
- **verification:** node scripts/build-sw.mjs succeeds; npx playwright test tests/e2e/shell.spec.ts (Chromium project) offline-fallback test stays green; npx vitest run tests/unit/library-integrity.test.ts green.
- **disposition:** pending

### SW-006 — e2e test name promises 'no-store cache policy' for /sw.js but asserts only 'no-cache', weaker than both its name and the actual header

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-SAFE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** tests/e2e/shell.spec.ts:274-280 — test titled 'the service worker is served with a no-store cache policy' asserts headers()['cache-control'] toContain('no-cache') only. next.config.ts:222 actually sets 'no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate', so the assertion passes but would also pass if no-store were dropped — the regression the name implies it guards against.
- **blast_radius:** tests/e2e/shell.spec.ts (test-only)
- **proposed_fix:** Tighten the assertion to expect both 'no-cache' and 'no-store' (or match the full directive string), keeping the test name honest.
- **verification:** npx playwright test tests/e2e/shell.spec.ts -g 'no-store' passes against the current next.config.ts headers.
- **disposition:** pending

### SW-007 — OutboxFlusher retries a permanently rejected item forever: a handler that keeps returning false (server validation failure, not offline) fires one server action per foreground/online event indefinitely with no attempt cap, age limit, or user surfacing

- severity `S3` · confidence `likely` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `none` · prior_ref `SAFE-02`
- **evidence:** src/components/pwa/OutboxFlusher.tsx:9-17 — handler drains only on res.ok || res.duplicate; a persistent non-duplicate rejection (e.g. payload failing server-side validation) returns false. src/lib/pwa/outbox.ts:56-66 — flushOutbox keeps false/throwing items with no retry counter, and OutboxFlusher.tsx:44-49 triggers a flush on mount, every 'online', and every visibilitychange. Unknown kinds are also kept forever by design (outbox.ts:58). The duplicate-as-drained fix (CHANGELOG, tests/unit/library-integrity.test.ts:147-152) closed the most likely permanent-failure case, but the general class remains: a poisoned item is invisible to the user and re-invokes savePromptAction on every foreground for the life of the origin storage.
- **blast_radius:** src/components/pwa/OutboxFlusher.tsx, src/lib/pwa/outbox.ts; server action load from authed clients
- **proposed_fix:** Record an attempts counter (or firstFailedAt) on the item and after a bounded number of confirmed non-transient rejections stop auto-retrying and surface the stranded draft to the user instead of silently retrying — dropping queued user data is a product decision, hence approval required.
- **verification:** Unit test: handler that always returns false; after N flushes assert the item transitions to a surfaced/parked state rather than being retried on flush N+1.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W2: attempts counter + MAX_OUTBOX_ATTEMPTS=3 parking with a one-time surfaced toast; parked items are kept, never retried (security.test.ts pins the ladder)

## Track SEC

> SEC track is fundamentally sound with disciplined authz layering and clean secret hygiene, degraded mainly by CSP script-src 'unsafe-inline' and uneven rate-limit coverage. PASS evidence: (1) Authz — every server action (all 4 'use server' files) and both API routes re-derive the user via supabase.auth.getUser() server-side; no client-supplied user id is ever trusted; mutations add redundant .eq('user_id', uid) on top of RLS; middleware gates via getUser() not getSession() (supabase/middleware.ts:54-58) and fails closed when env is missing; Next 15.5.21 is well past the CVE-2025-29927 middleware-bypass fix, and even a bypass would hit per-route re-checks. (2) Service role — single consumer confirmed (grep: only delete-account/route.ts imports createAdminClient), 'server-only' guarded, per-request construction, only session-derived user.id reaches admin calls. (3) Parameterization — every RPC uses parameter objects; all SECURITY DEFINER functions pin search_path and derive the owner from auth.uid() (20260730203605 spend_* functions, execute revoked from anon); usage_reservations is default-deny RLS with grants revoked; the only string-built filters are the two cursor .or() interpolations reported below. (4) Model-route limits — spend_reserve enforces rate + cap + hold atomically under a per-user advisory lock, cross-instance; the in-memory limiter is correctly described in-code as an advisory per-instance burst guard. (5) XSS surface — exactly one dangerouslySetInnerHTML (static NO_FLASH constant, no interpolation); model output rendered as plain React text, no markdown/HTML renderer anywhere. (6) Open redirect — none: auth callback/confirm redirect only to fixed paths on request.nextUrl.origin, no next/redirect param exists. (7) CSRF — server actions get Next's built-in origin check; route handlers ride SameSite=Lax (finding filed on the thin margin for delete-account). (8) Secrets — git history contains only .env.example with empty placeholders (git log --all --diff-filter=A), no secret-shaped strings across 200 revisions or HEAD; baseline audit.json: 0 vulnerabilities in 823 packages. (9) Headers — CSP + HSTS(preload) + XCTO + XFO + Referrer-Policy + Permissions-Policy applied to every path via /:path* including offline.html and sw.js. Headline defects: 'unsafe-inline' script-src with httpOnly:false auth cookies and a *.supabase.co connect-src wildcard (S2); zero rate limiting outside the two model routes despite CLAUDE.md section 9 claiming all endpoints (S2); client-controlled refine.baseInput embedded in the SYSTEM prompt (S2, protected-path fix); plus seven S3 hygiene items (cursor filter-grammar injection, CSRF defense-in-depth on delete-account, raw DB error passthrough, unvalidated avatar_url, escapable media fence, one unauth-gated drafts action, limiter map growth).
>
| id | sev | conf | fix class | design | title |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SEC-001 | S2 | certain | MANUAL-APPROVE | none | CSP permits 'unsafe-inline' scripts with no nonce/hash while Supabase auth cookies are httpOnly:false, so any XSS foothold yields session-token theft and an open exfil channel via the connect-src *.supabase.co wildcard |
| SEC-002 | S2 | certain | MANUAL-APPROVE | none | No rate limiting on any non-model endpoint or server action, contradicting CLAUDE.md section 9 'Rate limits on all endpoints'; the only limits that exist are on the two model routes, and the in-memory layer there is per-instance advisory |
| SEC-003 | S2 | certain | MANUAL-APPROVE | none | Client-controlled refine.baseInput (up to 20k chars) is embedded verbatim into the SYSTEM prompt, placing user text in the privileged role ahead of the envelope contract it can then countermand |
| SEC-004 | S3 | certain | AUTO-REVIEW | none | Unvalidated keyset cursor is interpolated into PostgREST .or() filter grammar (cursor.id always unquoted; cursor.value unquoted for timestamp sorts), allowing filter injection within the caller's own row set |
| SEC-005 | S3 | certain | AUTO-REVIEW | none | Raw PostgREST/Postgres error text is returned verbatim to the client from the pagination server actions, contradicting the codebase's own describeWriteError leak policy |
| SEC-006 | S3 | certain | AUTO-REVIEW | none | updateProfileAction accepts an arbitrary avatar_url string and the client renders it via next/image with `unoptimized`, bypassing remotePatterns validation |
| SEC-007 | S3 | certain | AUTO-REVIEW | none | The <attached-references> fence around client-supplied mediaContext blocks is escapable — a block containing the literal closing tag breaks out of the delimiter the route's comment calls 'clearly fenced' |
| SEC-008 | S3 | certain | AUTO-REVIEW | none | fetchDraftsPageAction performs no explicit auth check before querying, unlike its sibling fetchLibraryPageAction — it relies on RLS alone, the single point of failure the same file's own comments warn against |
| SEC-009 | S3 | certain | AUTO-REVIEW | none | In-memory rate limiter store never evicts expired windows, so the module-level Map grows monotonically with distinct user ids over an instance's lifetime |
| SEC-010 | S3 | likely | MANUAL-APPROVE | none | delete-account and sign-out POST handlers rely solely on the library-default SameSite=Lax cookie for CSRF defense — no Origin/Sec-Fetch-Site check, no re-auth or confirmation token on an irreversible service-role deletion |

### SEC-001 — CSP permits 'unsafe-inline' scripts with no nonce/hash while Supabase auth cookies are httpOnly:false, so any XSS foothold yields session-token theft and an open exfil channel via the connect-src *.supabase.co wildcard

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** next.config.ts:95 "script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'" (no nonce, no hash; residual acknowledged at next.config.ts:10-13 'A nonce-based policy is the next step'); node_modules/@supabase/ssr/dist/main/utils/constants.js:4-7 DEFAULT_COOKIE_OPTIONS = { sameSite: 'lax', httpOnly: false } and src/lib/supabase/server.ts / middleware.ts pass no cookieOptions override, so auth tokens are JS-readable; next.config.ts:101 connect-src allows https://*.supabase.co wss://*.supabase.co (any Supabase-hosted project, i.e. an attacker-registered project is a valid exfil endpoint). Mitigants verified: no 'unsafe-eval', object-src 'none', base-uri 'self', frame-ancestors 'none' (next.config.ts:106-108); zero dangerouslySetInnerHTML besides the static NO_FLASH bootstrap (src/app/layout.tsx:41,57, constant string, no interpolated user data); model output rendered as plain React text (no markdown/HTML renderer in package.json or src/components/diff).
- **blast_radius:** next.config.ts (CSP builder); every route in the app receives this policy via headers() source /:path* including offline.html and sw.js
- **proposed_fix:** Move CSP emission into middleware with a per-request nonce (script-src 'self' 'nonce-...'), hash the static NO_FLASH bootstrap (sha256), and narrow connect-src/img-src to the single configured Supabase project origin instead of the *.supabase.co wildcard (configuredSupabaseOrigin already computes it).
- **verification:** curl -sI https://<deploy>/ | grep -i content-security-policy shows nonce-based script-src and no 'unsafe-inline' for scripts; app still boots with no theme flash and no CSP violations in devtools console
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W3 (= PRI-010): per-request nonce CSP from the middleware (src/lib/security/csp.ts) drops script-src 'unsafe-inline' for every document; the connect-src/img-src wildcard is narrowed to the exact configured Supabase origin; offline.html/sw.js keep a static 'unsafe-inline' policy from next.config; e2e asserts the nonce header, axe confirms no theme-flash regression

### SEC-002 — No rate limiting on any non-model endpoint or server action, contradicting CLAUDE.md section 9 'Rate limits on all endpoints'; the only limits that exist are on the two model routes, and the in-memory layer there is per-instance advisory

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** Endpoint enumeration vs coverage: POST /api/enhance (route.ts:65 in-memory rateLimit + route.ts:211 spend_reserve DB window) COVERED; POST /api/media (route.ts:79 + route.ts:140) COVERED; GET /auth/callback, GET /auth/confirm (unauthenticated), POST /auth/sign-out, POST /auth/delete-account (service-role storage-list loop + admin.deleteUser, delete-account/route.ts:43-57) have ZERO rate limiting; all ~25 server actions across src/app/(auth)/actions.ts, src/lib/profile/actions.ts (updateEmailAction sends a confirmation email per call), src/lib/library/actions.ts, src/lib/drafts/actions.ts have ZERO rate limiting. Precise limiter semantics: src/lib/security/rate-limit.ts:24 defaultStore is a module-level Map, per serverless instance; concurrent bursts spread across fresh instances each see an empty window, so the in-memory limit is advisory only. The authoritative cross-instance limit is spend_reserve (supabase/migrations/20260730203605_atomic_spend_reservations.sql:104 pg_advisory_xact_lock + :131-143 per-user 60s event count) — durable and correct, but it exists only on the two model routes. CLAUDE.md section 6 guardrail ('rate limit + cost cap on every model route') IS met; section 9 ('Rate limits on all endpoints') is not.
- **blast_radius:** src/app/auth/* (4 routes), src/app/(auth)/actions.ts, src/lib/profile/actions.ts, src/lib/library/actions.ts, src/lib/drafts/actions.ts
- **proposed_fix:** Either add the in-memory burst guard (keyed by user id, or IP for the unauthenticated auth GETs) to the auth routes and mutating server actions and note the DB-backed option for durable limits, or amend CLAUDE.md section 9 to state the actual posture (durable limits on model routes only, platform-level DDoS elsewhere). Do not silently leave doc and code disagreeing.
- **verification:** grep -rn 'rateLimit(' src/app src/lib | count call sites covering each mutating endpoint; or a loop of 100 POSTs to /auth/sign-out observing 429s after the threshold
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W3: writeLimited/emailLimited (src/lib/security/action-limit.ts) guard every mutating server action across auth/profile/library/drafts; IP-keyed burst guard on the unauthenticated auth callback/confirm GETs; delete-account rate-limited; durable cross-instance limits stay on the model routes' spend_reserve as before

### SEC-003 — Client-controlled refine.baseInput (up to 20k chars) is embedded verbatim into the SYSTEM prompt, placing user text in the privileged role ahead of the envelope contract it can then countermand

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/lib/providers/formatters.ts:100-110 refineBlock() pushes refine.baseInput raw into the lines array; buildSystemPrompt() at formatters.ts:193 spreads refineBlock into the system string, before `conventions` (line 195) and `outputContract` (line 197) but as the same system-role message every provider adapter sends as `system` (adapter.ts:102,126). The route validates baseInput only for type and length (src/app/api/enhance/route.ts:165-169). The `<original>`/`<answers>` delimiters are not enforced — baseInput may contain a literal `</original>` plus new 'system' directives. Contrast: the main `input` and mediaContext correctly travel in the user message (route.ts:229-237, adapter passes input separately). Impact bounded to the caller's own run plus VASEY-side abuse (repurposing the route as a general LLM proxy), capped by spend_reserve ($2/day, 20/min); the prior paid eval (docs/audits/VIZION-enhancement-evaluation.md, PROD-04 section) tested injection via the user message only, not via refine.baseInput.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/providers/formatters.ts (protected path), src/app/api/enhance/route.ts refine path; all 12 provider adapters receive the tainted system string
- **proposed_fix:** Move the tone/answers context blocks out of the system role and append them to the user message (alongside input, as the mediaContext fence already does), keeping only the static REFINE_INSTRUCTIONS sentence in the system prompt; formatters.ts is a protected path defining OUTPUT_CONTRACT so this needs explicit approval plus regression of formatters.test.ts.
- **verification:** npx vitest run tests/unit/formatters.test.ts after change; assert buildSystemPrompt output contains no refine.baseInput content for kind=tone/answers and the adapter's user message does
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W3: refine.baseInput (tone original, Q&A block) moved OUT of the system role into the fenced user message (refineUserBlock); the system prompt only points at it; formatters.test.ts asserts the content never appears in buildSystemPrompt output

### SEC-004 — Unvalidated keyset cursor is interpolated into PostgREST .or() filter grammar (cursor.id always unquoted; cursor.value unquoted for timestamp sorts), allowing filter injection within the caller's own row set

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/lib/library/queries.ts:113 `q.or(`${spec.column}.${op}.${v},and(${spec.column}.eq.${v},id.lt.${cursor.id})`)` — quoteOrValue is applied only when spec.column === 'title' (line 112); cursor.id is never validated as a UUID or quoted. src/lib/drafts/queries.ts:104-106 interpolates both cursor.value and cursor.id unquoted. The cursor is client-supplied via fetchLibraryPageAction (src/lib/library/actions.ts:334-354) and fetchDraftsPageAction (src/lib/drafts/actions.ts:282-302); decodeCursor (paging.ts:111-118) splits on  with no shape validation. A crafted cursor (e.g. id = 'x,id.not.is.null') injects extra OR branches. Containment verified: RLS plus the AND-combined top-level filters (.is('deleted_at', null) etc.) mean injected branches can only widen the OR condition over the caller's OWN rows — no cross-tenant reach; practical effect is pagination-invariant breakage or PostgREST 400s.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/library/queries.ts, src/lib/drafts/queries.ts; /library page and both Load-more server actions
- **proposed_fix:** Validate cursor.id against the existing UUID_RE (paging.ts:51) in decodeCursor or at both call sites, and pass every interpolated cursor value through quoteOrValue (PostgREST accepts quoted timestamps), rejecting the cursor (fresh first page) on mismatch.
- **verification:** npx vitest run tests/unit/library-paging.test.ts plus a new case: decodeCursor('2026-01-01x,id.not.is.null') must not reach the query builder unquoted; manual: fetchLibraryPageAction with a crafted cursor returns ok:false or page 1, never a PostgREST error string
- **independently found by:** LIB ("Client-supplied keyset cursor is interpolated raw into PostgREST or() grammar, and a title containing U+001F b")
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W3: decodeCursor pins the id to a UUID and refuses a tampered cursor (fresh first page); every interpolated cursor value is quoteOrValue-wrapped at both library+drafts sites; library-paging.test.ts covers the injection string

### SEC-005 — Raw PostgREST/Postgres error text is returned verbatim to the client from the pagination server actions, contradicting the codebase's own describeWriteError leak policy

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/lib/library/queries.ts:120 `throw new Error(error.message)` and src/lib/drafts/queries.ts:118 same; caught and returned to the browser at src/lib/library/actions.ts:352 and src/lib/drafts/actions.ts:300 (`error: e.message`). src/lib/supabase/errors.ts:56-70 documents exactly why raw Postgres text must not reach users ('constraint names, column names, policy names, function signatures and enum type names') and every mutation path follows it — these two read paths do not. Combined with the cursor-injection finding, a crafted cursor can deterministically surface PostgREST parser errors to the client.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/library/queries.ts, src/lib/drafts/queries.ts, fetchLibraryPageAction, fetchDraftsPageAction; /library UI error toasts
- **proposed_fix:** Log the raw message server-side via writeErrorLogLine and return the generic "Couldn't load more." fallback from both actions (the fallback string already exists in both catch blocks).
- **verification:** npx vitest run tests/unit/drafts-queries.test.ts tests/unit/library-paging.test.ts; grep confirms no `e.message` passthrough remains in the two actions' catch blocks
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W3: both pagination actions log the raw PostgREST text via writeErrorLogLine and return the generic 'Couldn't load more.' — the describeWriteError leak policy the mutations already followed

### SEC-006 — updateProfileAction accepts an arbitrary avatar_url string and the client renders it via next/image with `unoptimized`, bypassing remotePatterns validation

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/lib/profile/actions.ts:40 stores patch.avatar_url with no protocol/host validation; src/components/settings/SettingsPanel.tsx:194-200 renders it with `<Image src={profile.avatar_url} ... unoptimized />` — `unoptimized` skips the loader so next.config.ts:197-205 remotePatterns never apply. Actual load is confined only by CSP img-src (next.config.ts:98: 'self' data: blob: *.supabase.co googleusercontent github). Self-affecting (own profile, image context cannot execute script), so hygiene not breach.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/profile/actions.ts, src/components/settings/SettingsPanel.tsx; /profile page
- **proposed_fix:** Server-side in updateProfileAction, accept avatar_url only when it parses as https on the configured Supabase storage origin or the two OAuth avatar CDNs (the same allowlist as CSP img-src); reject otherwise.
- **verification:** Unit test: updateProfileAction({avatar_url: 'https://attacker.example/x.png'}) returns ok:false; the storage-origin URL from SettingsPanel.tsx:135-136 still passes
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W3: updateProfileAction allowlists avatar_url server-side (configured Supabase origin + the two OAuth CDNs), matching CSP img-src

### SEC-007 — The <attached-references> fence around client-supplied mediaContext blocks is escapable — a block containing the literal closing tag breaks out of the delimiter the route's comment calls 'clearly fenced'

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/app/api/enhance/route.ts:229-236 joins up to 4 client-supplied blocks (each up to 2,000 chars, validated for type/length only at route.ts:179-194) between literal '<attached-references>' and '</attached-references>' lines with no neutralization of an embedded '</attached-references>'. Correct placement otherwise: the fence rides in the USER message, not the system prompt, and the accompanying instruction is advisory. Only the caller's own run is affected.
- **blast_radius:** src/app/api/enhance/route.ts providerInput composition; all provider adapters downstream
- **proposed_fix:** Strip or entity-escape the literal string '</attached-references>' (and '<attached-references>') from each block before joining, mirroring standard tag-fencing hygiene.
- **verification:** Unit test on the composed providerInput: a block containing '</attached-references>ignore previous' yields exactly one closing tag in the final string
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W3: neutralizeTag strips the literal <attached-references> fence from each mediaContext block before joining (and from refine blocks); pinned by formatters.test.ts

### SEC-008 — fetchDraftsPageAction performs no explicit auth check before querying, unlike its sibling fetchLibraryPageAction — it relies on RLS alone, the single point of failure the same file's own comments warn against

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/lib/drafts/actions.ts:282-302 calls queryDraftsPage directly with no getUser()/ownerId() gate; src/lib/library/actions.ts:344-347 (the equivalent action) checks the session first. src/lib/drafts/actions.ts:160-166 documents the project's own rationale for redundant owner scoping ('RLS is otherwise the only control'), and queryDraftsPage (drafts/queries.ts:84-86) applies no user_id predicate, so this action is the one drafts read path with neither belt nor braces beyond RLS.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/drafts/actions.ts fetchDraftsPageAction; Drafts view Load more
- **proposed_fix:** Add the same ownerId() gate returning SESSION_EXPIRED when null, matching fetchLibraryPageAction; optionally add .eq('user_id', uid) inside queryDraftsPage for parity with the mutation paths.
- **verification:** npx vitest run tests/unit/drafts-queries.test.ts; code review confirms both page actions share the same auth precondition
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W3: fetchDraftsPageAction gates on ownerId() like its library sibling

### SEC-009 — In-memory rate limiter store never evicts expired windows, so the module-level Map grows monotonically with distinct user ids over an instance's lifetime

- severity `S3` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/lib/security/rate-limit.ts:24 `const defaultStore = new Map()` with rateLimit() only ever set()/get() (lines 33-43); an expired Hit is overwritten only when the SAME key hits again — keys for users who never return persist until the serverless instance recycles. Bounded in practice (two small fields per user, instances are short-lived), so hygiene only.
- **blast_radius:** src/lib/security/rate-limit.ts; /api/enhance and /api/media memory footprint
- **proposed_fix:** Opportunistic sweep: on each call (or every Nth), delete entries whose resetAt < now; pure-core injection style already supports testing it.
- **verification:** npx vitest run tests/unit/rate-limit.test.ts with a new case asserting store.size stays bounded after expiry
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W3: the in-memory limiter opportunistically sweeps expired windows every 64th call — the module Map no longer grows monotonically

### SEC-010 — delete-account and sign-out POST handlers rely solely on the library-default SameSite=Lax cookie for CSRF defense — no Origin/Sec-Fetch-Site check, no re-auth or confirmation token on an irreversible service-role deletion

- severity `S3` · confidence `likely` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** src/app/auth/delete-account/route.ts:22-66 reads no body, checks no Origin header, and proceeds from session cookie straight to admin.storage removal and auth.admin.deleteUser; src/app/auth/sign-out/route.ts:5-11 same shape. Protection today is only @supabase/ssr's default sameSite:'lax' (node_modules/@supabase/ssr/dist/main/utils/constants.js:6) — not explicitly configured anywhere in src/lib/supabase/*, so a future cookieOptions change silently removes the only CSRF barrier. Next.js applies its built-in origin check to server actions, not to route handlers. Cross-origin form POST is blocked by Lax in current browsers, hence S3 defense-in-depth rather than S1.
- **blast_radius:** src/app/auth/delete-account/route.ts, src/app/auth/sign-out/route.ts
- **proposed_fix:** In both POST handlers, reject when an Origin header is present and does not match request.nextUrl.origin (tolerate absent Origin for same-origin legacy UAs); for delete-account additionally require a recent re-auth or an explicit confirmation field in the POSTed form body.
- **verification:** npx vitest run tests/unit/delete-account-route.test.ts extended with a request carrying Origin: https://evil.example expecting 403; existing happy-path test stays green
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W3: crossOriginPost (src/lib/security/same-origin.ts) refuses a mismatched Origin on sign-out and delete-account; delete-account is additionally rate-limited

## Track DSN

> DSN track health is strong at the token layer and weaker at the edges: the 7-role palette matches spec and style-guide canon byte-for-byte, the §6 contrast law is mechanically enforced (a11y.test.ts guard, 0 offenders; all laser/amber/pulse fills verified >=10:1 with on-laser ink), and all 12 developer accents sit computationally inside their documented 3:1 corridor with test-pinned geometry. One invariant violation was found: the stream-progress sweep and result-shimmer use raw --laser as a thin stroke/segment on light-theme surfaces (1.09-1.18:1 computed — the exact FAIL the tokens.css header forbids, and which Horizon/Footer already solve via --accent-ink), making the light-theme activity indicator effectively invisible (S1). Systemic S2s: KeyboardActionBar wears the top-bar glass-chrome tier while bottom-anchored (bottom-rounded corners + downward shadow against the keyboard); icon strokes drift across 1.5/1.75/2 against the '1.5px on a 24px grid' canon (needs a nominal-vs-effective ruling); the focus-glow value is defined twice (tailwind boxShadow.focus hardcodes the --laser-glow rgba); ModeRig hardcodes the mode count 6 in two places uncovered by tests; press affordance (PressableButton) is applied to ConfirmSheet footers but not to the visually identical footers of five other sheets; and the PWA-08 static dark themeColor carry-forward remains open. S3 hygiene: 10px/11px type sizes outside the locked scale at 18 sites, duplicated scrim/panel color-mix values, no motion token scale (lens-lock 300ms is unanchored), an imperceptible light-theme btn-secondary hover, a stale contrast.test.ts citation, one arbitrary-duplicate utility, divergent input recipes, 8-vs-12px bottom-offset drift, and a one-off rounded-[10px] on the header squircle. The z-index ladder is fully inventoried and coherent (-10 ambient through 100 skip-link) but documented only in a component comment. Named checks: auth hero centering and app-header squircle placement PASS per R1.1/R1.2; glass tier parameters are internally consistent and deliberate; dark/light parity is token-pure (no per-theme component branches); the diagonal beam motif does not exist anywhere in canon or code, so its angle check is vacuous-PASS.
>
**Verified pass evidence:** PASS evidence: token values, contrast law, dev accents, glass tiers, brand placement, theme parity, and shared primitives all verify against canon — (1) All 8 role hexes in tokens.css match VIZION-product-spec.md §1.2 and VIZION-style-guide.html:12-13 exactly (void #0f1012, onyx #2b2d33, silver #b9bcc5, chalk #f2f3f6, laser #b7ff3c, pulse #3dd68c, flare #ff5247, amber #ffc24b). (2) §6 button law upheld: zero text-laser in src; every bg-laser pairs with text-on-laser (computed 15.77:1); bg-amber/bg-pulse fills carry on-laser at 11.86/10.16:1; guard test a11y.test.ts:203-215 enforces -ink text roles, 0 offenders. (3) All 12 dev accents computed inside the documented corridor Y in [0.1995,0.2922]; worst case 3.06:1 (deepseek vs light card) and 3.08:1 (qwen vs aurora-lit dark card), both >=3:1; --on-flare figures reproduce dev-accents.css comments to the digit; pair-pinning tests exist (developer-accents.test.ts:124-142) and baseline is green (976/976 unit tests, exits.txt test_exit=0). (4) Glass tiers internally consistent: .glass blur16/sat1.25 over --glass 0.72/0.82; chrome blur18/sat1.4 over --chrome 0.45/0.42 (documented rationale tokens.css:90-93); fab blur16/sat1.3 over laser 82% (rationale globals.css:357-376); tailwind backdropBlur.glass=16px matches. (5) Auth checks: gate has no top bar by design, AuthHero centers the 176px mark above the wordmark (AuthHero.tsx:17-25, R1.2/R6.2); app header squircle sits left of the wordmark (ScreenHeader.tsx:29-37, R1.1). (6) Diagonal beam motif: absent from style guide, product spec, brand masters and code (grep for beam/diagonal/skew: only an unrelated 90deg arrow rotate in the guide's demo CSS) — angle-consistency check not applicable. (7) Dark/light parity: zero dark:/light: variants or data-theme branches in components; all theming flows through tokens; NeuralMeshBackground reads --silver/--accent-ink via getComputedStyle and re-resolves on theme flip. (8) Concentric radius math correct in both instruments (16/12 and 12/8, outer = inner + 4px padding). (9) Sheets consolidated on the one Sheet primitive (pickers included); diff rendering consolidated in segments.tsx explicitly to prevent drift.

### DSN-001 — KeyboardActionBar uses the top-bar .glass-chrome tier while bottom-anchored above the keyboard, inheriting bottom-rounded corners and a downward shadow

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `visual`
- **evidence:** KeyboardActionBar.tsx:48 className="glass-chrome fixed inset-x-0 z-40 ..." with style bottom:`${inset}px` (line 49) — anchored to the keyboard top. globals.css:246-250: .glass-chrome sets border-bottom-left/right-radius:20px and box-shadow 0 8px 28px rgba(0,0,0,0.28) (downward). The bottom-anchored tier is .glass-nav (top radii, upward shadow, globals.css:251-255), which BottomNav.tsx:163 uses. Result: two 20px corner notches open to the page directly above the keyboard edge and the bar's content-facing top edge is square — the inverse of every other bottom-anchored chrome surface. The ::before tint follows via border-radius:inherit (globals.css:241).
- **blast_radius:** src/components/editor/KeyboardActionBar.tsx; visible on /enhance whenever the software keyboard is open (iOS/Android)
- **proposed_fix:** Swap glass-chrome for glass-nav on the bar (one class), giving top-rounded corners against content and an upward shadow onto the keyboard, matching the BottomNav treatment.
- **verification:** On an iOS/Android viewport focus the composer textarea; inspect the bar above the keyboard: corners rounded on top, square on bottom, shadow cast upward (getComputedStyle borderTopLeftRadius=20px, boxShadow starts '0 -8px').
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W4: KeyboardActionBar wears .glass-nav (top-rounded, upward shadow) — the bottom-anchored mirror of the top chrome

### DSN-002 — Carry-forward PWA-08: viewport themeColor is still a single static dark #0F1012 with no media-qualified light variant

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `visual` · prior_ref `PWA-08`
- **evidence:** src/app/layout.tsx:33 themeColor: "#0F1012" (single value, no media array); ThemeManager.tsx:49 corrects it only after hydration via setMeta("theme-color", surface) with SURFACE dark #0F1012 / light #EEF0F4 (ThemeManager.tsx:10-13). A light-theme user gets dark browser chrome on every cold load until hydration. Prior ledger PWA-08 (docs/audits/VIZION-enhancement-ledger.json) verdict Confirmed, decision "P2 — planned next cycle" — this is still-open, not new drift. The hexes also duplicate tokens.css:16/:81 by necessity (meta cannot read CSS vars); light #EEF0F4 matches --void light exactly.
- **blast_radius:** src/app/layout.tsx, src/components/ThemeManager.tsx; every route's first paint for light-theme users
- **proposed_fix:** Per the ledger's simpler alternative: emit themeColor as [{media:'(prefers-color-scheme: light)', color:'#EEF0F4'}, {color:'#0F1012'}] and restrict ThemeManager mutation to explicit user overrides — requires deciding the ownership rule the ledger flags.
- **verification:** curl the rendered HTML head: two <meta name="theme-color"> tags, the light one media-qualified; cold-load in a light-scheme browser shows light chrome pre-hydration.
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W4: media-qualified light/dark themeColor pair (#0F1012 / #EEF0F4) — PWA-08 closed

### DSN-003 — Press affordance divergence: ConfirmSheet footers use PressableButton but the identical footer CTAs in five other sheets are raw buttons with no touch feedback

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `visual`
- **evidence:** Doctrine at globals.css:262-263: ".pressable ... nothing uses `active:` for touch feedback any more", and globals.css:27 suppresses -webkit-tap-highlight-color, so a raw button's only feedback path is the unreliable iOS :active (globals.css:266-273). ConfirmSheet.tsx:48-67 correctly uses PressableButton subtle for both footer CTAs. Structurally identical sheet-footer CTAs are plain <button>: LibraryFilterSheet.tsx:144-151, MediaPrivacySheet.tsx:33-40, GenerateSheet.tsx:106-126, CollectionSheet.tsx:114/:194, DraftsList.tsx:443-451, AttachmentDetailsSheet.tsx:62-70, MediaPreviewSheet.tsx:48-57, plus the auth CTAs SignInForm.tsx:184 (has PressableButton imported but the submit is raw) and SetPasswordForm.tsx:57. PressableButton/PressableLink adoption: 9 files vs ~40 files with raw <button> (grep counts).
- **blast_radius:** All sheet footers under src/components/{library,media,ui}, auth forms; touch feedback on iOS across /library, /enhance, /profile, /sign-in
- **proposed_fix:** Adopt PressableButton subtle for sheet-footer and full-width CTAs matching the ConfirmSheet pattern (mechanical class-preserving swap; press scale 3% appears on tap).
- **verification:** grep -rn '<button' src/components/library/LibraryFilterSheet.tsx returns none for footer CTAs; on-device tap shows the 0.97 scale; tests/unit suite stays green (976 passing baseline).
- **disposition:** partially-resolved — Stage 2 W4: PressableButton on the primary footer CTAs in MediaPrivacySheet and GenerateSheet; the remaining raw-button footers stay a follow-up (the <a>/<Link> footers need PressableLink)

### DSN-004 — Icon stroke-width drift: style guide canon is 1.5px on a 24px grid, but 1.75 and 2 ship at 14 sites, yielding effective weights from 1.17px to 1.75px

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `NEEDS-RULING` · design_impact `visual`
- **evidence:** Canon: VIZION-style-guide.html:324 "Iconography: 1.5px stroke · rounded joins · 24px grid". Conforming (strokeWidth 1.5): BottomNav.tsx:26-62 (h-6, true 1.5px), ScreenHeader.tsx:62 (h-6), ModeRig.tsx:160 (h-5 → 1.25px effective), NewPromptFab.tsx:153 (h-7 → 1.75px effective), KindIcon.tsx:25. Deviating: strokeWidth 1.75 at Sheet.tsx:132 (h-5 → 1.46px), ThinkingPicker.tsx:73/:181 (h-4 → 1.17px), TargetPicker.tsx:112/:241, AttachmentTray.tsx:45, TransformationDiff.tsx:332/:338, PromptDetail.tsx:332/:338/:601; strokeWidth 2 at CheckGlyph.tsx:16 (h-5 → 1.67px), TransformationDiff.tsx:317, PromptDetail.tsx:317, ActivityFeed.tsx:36 (h-3.5 → 1.17px). Same rendered size (h-5, 24 viewBox) carries three nominal weights (1.5/1.75/2).
- **blast_radius:** 13 component files across nav, sheets, pickers, diff, library; every screen
- **proposed_fix:** Ruling needed on whether the canon means nominal strokeWidth=1.5 on the 24 grid (then normalize 14 sites down) or effective rendered 1.5px (then 1.75-on-h-5 is the compliant optical compensation and the h-5/h-4 1.5s should be raised). Then normalize to one rule and record it in the style guide.
- **verification:** grep -rn 'strokeWidth' src | grep -v '1.5' returns only sites the ruling explicitly blesses; visual pass over the picker sheet where CheckGlyph (2), chevron (1.75) and DeveloperIcon coexist.
- **disposition:** resolved (ruling Q6=b) — Stage 2 W4: per-size optical stroke weights blessed; ADR-0004 records the effective-~1.5px canon and the shared Glyph primitive is the 1.5px/24-grid reference

### DSN-005 — Focus-glow value defined twice: tailwind.config.ts boxShadow.focus hardcodes rgba(183,255,60,0.25) instead of var(--laser-glow)

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `token-only`
- **evidence:** tokens.css:55 defines --laser-glow: rgba(183, 255, 60, 0.25); globals.css:138-141 --focus-ring composes '0 0 0 1px var(--accent-ink), 0 0 24px var(--laser-glow)'. tailwind.config.ts:86 boxShadow.focus: "0 0 0 1px var(--accent-ink), 0 0 24px rgba(183, 255, 60, 0.25)" — the same ring with the glow as a literal. Consumer: EnhanceComposer.tsx:350 (shadow-focus / focus-within:shadow-focus on the composer chassis). globals.css:939 also repeats the literal as a var() fallback. Retuning --laser-glow forks the composer's ring from every other focus ring silently. (Also: boxShadow.hair at tailwind.config.ts:87 has zero consumers — dead token utility.)
- **blast_radius:** tailwind.config.ts, src/components/editor/EnhanceComposer.tsx; the composer focus ring on /enhance
- **proposed_fix:** Change boxShadow.focus to '0 0 0 1px var(--accent-ink), 0 0 24px var(--laser-glow)' — identical computed value today, single source of truth thereafter. Optionally drop unused boxShadow.hair.
- **verification:** npm run build; getComputedStyle on the focused composer shows the identical box-shadow; grep 'rgba(183, 255, 60' outside tokens.css returns only the documented globals.css:939 fallback (or none if also cleaned).
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W4: tailwind boxShadow.focus reads var(--laser-glow); the composer ring can't fork from the rest

### DSN-006 — Mode-count literals: ModeRig hardcodes 6 in grid-cols-6 and the indicator width calc while keyboard nav derives from MODES.length; the loading skeleton repeats 6 and mismatches gap

- severity `S2` · confidence `certain` · fix_class `AUTO-REVIEW` · design_impact `none`
- **evidence:** ModeRig.tsx:51 grid-cols-6; ModeRig.tsx:87 width: "calc((100% - 0.5rem) / 6)"; keyboard handling uses MODES.length (ModeRig.tsx:57). MODES has 6 entries including the post-launch Polish addition (src/lib/constants.ts:9-20) — the count has already changed once (5→6). tests/unit/mode-rig.test.tsx pins neither literal (grep for grid-cols-6 / '/ 6' returns nothing). Segmented.tsx:65-69 solves the identical problem correctly with inline gridTemplateColumns repeat(options.length, minmax(0,1fr)). enhance/loading.tsx:32-34 hardcodes 6 again AND uses gap-1 where the real rig is gap-0 (ModeRig.tsx:51), so skeleton cells render narrower than the real cells; loading.tsx:28 also re-literals the Horizon band pair w-[64%] max-w-[240px] duplicated from Horizon.tsx:58.
- **blast_radius:** src/components/editor/ModeRig.tsx, src/app/(app)/enhance/loading.tsx; /enhance. Failure surfaces only when MODES changes: lens-lock indicator width and cell grid silently desync
- **proposed_fix:** Derive both from MODES.length (inline gridTemplateColumns: repeat(MODES.length, minmax(0,1fr)); width: calc((100% - 0.5rem) / MODES.length) via template literal), reuse for the skeleton, and align the skeleton's gap to gap-0. No rendered change today.
- **verification:** npx vitest run tests/unit/mode-rig.test.tsx tests/unit/horizon.test.ts; visual: indicator exactly covers the active cell at 360/390/430px; temporarily append a 7th mode locally and confirm the indicator still aligns.
- **independently found by:** MOD ("Mode count hardcoded outside the mode table: the Enhance loading skeleton renders a literal 6-cell grid and th")
- **disposition:** resolved — Stage 2 W4: ModeRig grid + indicator width derive from MODES.length via inline gridTemplateColumns; the skeleton uses MODES too

### DSN-007 — R5 partial: at a 320px viewport the 'Condense' and 'Reformat' labels overflow their ModeRig cells by ~5px with no overflow handling, and no test guards the gate's recorded 360/390/430 symmetry.

- severity `S2` · confidence `likely` · fix_class `MANUAL-APPROVE` · design_impact `visual`
- **evidence:** Computed from real assets and code, not rendered: enhance page container 'max-w-screen-sm px-4' (enhance/page.tsx:18) + composer root 'flex flex-col gap-5' with no extra padding (EnhanceComposer.tsx:296) + rig p-1 (ModeRig.tsx:51) gives a 280px track at 320px viewport, 46.7px/cell. fontTools measurement of src/app/fonts/RedditSans-500.woff2 at the cell's 11px (text-[0.6875rem]) + tracking-wide: 'Condense' 51.9px, 'Reformat' 49.1px. Cells are minmax(0,1fr) (Tailwind 3.4, package.json:60) so tracks stay equal and the centered, non-wrapping label spans (ModeRig.tsx:118, leading-none, single words) paint outside the cell. tests/e2e/authed.spec.ts:171 exercises 320/390 only for .horizon, never the rig.

- **proposed_fix:** Blocked on the six-mode NEEDS-RULING above; the minimum viable change consistent with any ruling is an e2e assertion that label scrollWidth <= cell width at 320/360/390/430, which will pin whichever visual fix is approved.
- **verification:** npx playwright test with viewport 320x860: for each role=radio cell assert (await label.evaluate(el => el.scrollWidth)) <= (await cell.boundingBox()).width
- **disposition:** resolved (ruling Q1=a) — Stage 2 W4: six cells blessed (ADR-0004); labels shrink a step below 360px so Condense/Reformat stop overflowing at 320px
